# Angel Learning Intelligence (ALI) — Decision Log

Permanent architectural history of ALI. Every entry below was a real decision made during the ALI design/planning process (2026-07-02). This log is appended to, never rewritten — if a past decision is later reversed, add a new entry that supersedes it and note the supersession on the old entry, rather than editing the old entry's content away.

---

### Decision 1 — Evolve from fixed mock papers to an adaptive assessment engine
**Decision:** Replace the static, offset-sliced mock system with a dynamically assembled adaptive engine — no two mocks identical.
**Rationale:** Real beta feedback (from Angel) identified that fixed static mocks are a structural ceiling — every student on a pathway sees the exact same question slice every time (`MOCK_CONFIGS` in `app/mocks/[pathway]/page.tsx`).
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Triggered the full architecture proposal (`ADAPTIVE_ASSESSMENT_ENGINE_ARCHITECTURE.md`) — Master Question Bank, Mock Assembly Engine, difficulty progression, anti-repetition, personalised composition, analytics integration, scalability.

### Decision 2 — Between-mock adaptivity only; no live within-mock difficulty changes
**Decision:** Difficulty is fixed for a whole mock at assembly time and re-evaluated after completion. No live server round-trips inside a running exam session.
**Rationale:** The current mock runner is fully client-side and stable; introducing live difficulty changes would require server round-trips mid-exam, a bigger architectural lift with real regression risk to a working feature.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** No live-adaptivity hooks exist anywhere in the design. Within-mock (CAT-style) adaptivity remains a possible future slice, explicitly out of scope until reconsidered.

### Decision 3 — Hand-tag all 52 Verbal Reasoning questions; do not automate metadata generation
**Decision:** Every question's metadata (`content_difficulty`, `skill`, `estimated_time_seconds`, etc.) is assigned by careful human review, not generated programmatically.
**Rationale:** Metadata quality directly drives selection/difficulty/mastery logic; treated as long-term infrastructure that deserves the same rigor as the questions themselves.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `QUESTION_AUTHORING_STANDARD.md` (Decision 14) defines the process and template but assigns no real tags itself, beyond 3 explicitly-labeled illustrative worked examples. The 52-question tagging pass is a separate, human-owned task, decoupled from code development via Decision 5.

### Decision 4 — Question-count-based cooldown, tiered by difficulty
**Decision:** Anti-repetition cooldown is measured in intervening questions presented to the student, not elapsed calendar time. Thresholds: Easy≈5, Medium≈10, Hard≈15, Challenge≈20+ (20 is a floor, raisable).
**Rationale:** Behavior must stay consistent regardless of how frequently a student studies — a student doing 3 mocks in one afternoon and one doing 3 mocks over 3 weeks should see the same repetition behavior.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Required a new per-student monotonic sequence counter (`ali_student_adaptive_state.questions_presented_count`) and a `last_presented_at_sequence` stamp on each history row — a materially different mechanism than a simple `last_presented_at` timestamp comparison.

### Decision 5 — Reasoning-subject enum additions, additive-only
**Decision:** `subject_type` (Postgres enum, migration 001) gains 4 new values (`verbal-reasoning`, `non-verbal-reasoning`, `spatial-reasoning`, `numerical-reasoning`) via `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE`. Existing 5 values are never modified.
**Rationale:** These subjects are already first-class throughout the app's `SkillType`/`MockConfig` logic but were never added to the DB-level enum from migration 001 — a pre-existing gap, not a new requirement.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Confirms the general migration principle (Decision 16) applies even to enum changes — additive only, never destructive, even for a gap-fill.

### Decision 6 — Expanded question metadata, some fields deliberately inert this slice
**Decision:** `ali_question_bank` carries `confidence_weight`, `learning_objective`, `revision_priority`, `mastery_threshold` in addition to the original core fields, even though the first slice's logic doesn't use several of them yet.
**Rationale:** Design for the shared-layer future (Decision 8) without over-building — capture fields now that are cheap to add to a schema but expensive to retrofit later (a missing column means re-tagging 52+ questions twice).
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `confidence_weight` ships at default `1.00`, unused until confidence computation migrates to read from ALI directly (a future slice). `learning_objective`/`revision_priority` are optional, safe to backfill. Explicitly flagged in the plan so "field exists" is never mistaken for "field is wired into logic."

### Decision 7 — Mastery is evidence-based across distinct sessions
**Decision:** A question is `mastered` only after correct answers in `mastery_threshold` distinct sessions, with the most recent attempt also correct (one wrong answer demotes mastered → learning).
**Rationale:** A single correct answer, or several correct answers within one sitting, isn't reliable evidence of real understanding — genuine mastery should be demonstrated repeatedly, over time.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `ali_student_question_history` tracks `distinct_correct_sessions` and `last_correct_session_id`, not just a running correct-count. Mastery is revocable, not a permanent badge.

### Decision 8 — Adopt the internal architecture name "Angel Learning Intelligence (ALI)"
**Decision:** The shared adaptive intelligence layer is named ALI internally. It is designed to eventually power lessons, practice, daily missions, replay, confidence, readiness, and Parent Insights — the adaptive mock engine is one consumer of ALI, not the entire system. Internal engineering name only, never user-facing.
**Rationale:** Prevents the schema/module design from being accidentally mock-specific — naming the layer forces every design choice to ask "does this generalize beyond mocks?"
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Table naming convention (`ali_` prefix), module layout (`lib/ali/*` shared, `lib/adaptiveMockBuilder.ts` as one consumer), and the `source` tag on every history write (open string, not closed to `'adaptive_mock'`) all derive directly from this decision.

### Decision 9 — First implementation slice: GL pathway, Verbal Reasoning only
**Decision:** Slice 1 = GL pathway, VR bank only (~52 questions), full ALI pipeline, full analytics write-back, complete isolation from the existing production mock system.
**Rationale:** Smallest slice that still exercises the entire pipeline end-to-end on real content, rather than a toy example — proves the architecture without betting the whole question bank on an unproven design.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** NVR/NR/Vocabulary sections of the GL mock continue using the existing static-slice logic this slice. Every other pathway is untouched.

### Decision 10 — Mastery thresholds are configurable, not hard-coded
**Decision:** Difficulty→mastery-threshold defaults live in a dedicated table (`ali_mastery_defaults`), read at question-import time, not as a constant in application code.
**Rationale:** Tuning mastery sensitivity should not require a code deploy — it's a content/pedagogy calibration, not an engineering change.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Per-question overrides (via hand-tagging, per `QUESTION_AUTHORING_STANDARD.md` §8) still take precedence over the config default. Changing the config table only affects future imports, not retroactively re-tagging already-imported questions.

### Decision 11 — Weak-skill override never resurfaces the immediately preceding mock's questions
**Decision:** A question presented in a student's most recent mock can never reappear in their next mock, even under the weak-skill override that otherwise lets cooling-down questions resurface early.
**Rationale:** An earlier heuristic (`distance < count`) approximated this but could theoretically be violated at small bank sizes; the exact rule removes any ambiguity.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Implemented as an absolute step-1 exclusion in `selectQuestions()` (§3.2 of the implementation plan) — computed as the exact set of questions sharing the student's most recent sequence stamp, removed before any cooldown or override logic runs. Structurally impossible to violate, not just guarded against.

### Decision 12 — `ali_` table prefix and `lib/ali/` module convention
**Decision:** All new ALI tables are prefixed `ali_`; shared logic lives under `lib/ali/`, consumed by `lib/adaptiveMockBuilder.ts`.
**Rationale:** Makes the architectural boundary (Decision 8) visible directly in the schema and file tree, not just in documentation.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Any future ALI consumer (a lesson, a quiz) imports from `lib/ali/*` without needing to know anything about mocks.

### Decision 13 — `skill` uses a fine-grained competency taxonomy, not the app's existing `SkillType`
**Decision:** `ali_question_bank.skill` is populated from a new competency taxonomy (e.g. `vr.analogies`, `vr.letter-codes`) derived from the real `category` field already present on each question, not from the app's existing `SkillType` union.
**Rationale:** Discovered while writing `QUESTION_AUTHORING_STANDARD.md` that `SkillType` is uniformly `"verbal-reasoning"` for every VR question — zero sub-skill granularity. Reusing it would have made skill-level weak-question detection (and the Decision 11 override) unable to distinguish "weak at codes" from "weak at analogies," silently defeating the point of skill-level history.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `QUESTION_AUTHORING_STANDARD.md` §3 defines the full taxonomy (10 competency codes derived from real `category` values already in `data/verbal-reasoning/*.ts`). This is a correction to earlier plan drafts (v1/v2 assumed `SkillType` reuse), caught before any code was written against the wrong assumption.

### Decision 14 — `QUESTION_AUTHORING_STANDARD.md` as a permanent standard
**Decision:** A standing document governs metadata definitions, competency definitions, difficulty definitions, writing standards, UK English guidance, originality requirements, copyright guidance, and mastery guidance for every question authored from this point forward — not just the first 52.
**Rationale:** A future contributor should be able to write a question indistinguishable in quality/structure from one written today, without re-deriving judgment calls each time.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Governs all future subjects (Non-Verbal/Spatial/Numerical Reasoning, Maths, English, etc.) when they're hand-tagged in future slices — those subjects need their own competency-table addition to §3, built the same way (from real existing category/type distinctions, not invented).

### Decision 15 — Subject-level difficulty for Slice 1, not competency-level
**Decision:** Slice 1's difficulty-tier distribution operates at subject granularity (all VR competencies share one confidence tier), not per-competency. Competency-level difficulty is deliberately deferred.
**Rationale:** Prove the architecture first with a smaller, well-understood scope before extending `computeSubjectConfidence()` to finer granularity — avoids scope creep in the first slice while still passing all stated validation criteria (subject-level confidence is real; weak-skill revisiting works independently via `buildReplayQueue()`, which is already skill-granular).
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `confidenceBySkill` in `buildAdaptiveMock()` assigns the same tier to every VR competency code this slice. A future slice extending `computeSubjectConfidence()` to competency granularity would remove this limitation without changing the selection/mastery mechanisms themselves.

### Decision 16 — Migrations are additive, isolated, and reversible
**Decision:** Every ALI migration only adds new tables/enum values; never alters or drops anything from migrations 001–003. New route, new result type — no shared code path with the existing mock runner. Rollback = drop new tables/route, nothing to reverse in existing data.
**Rationale:** "Maintain production stability throughout" — the existing mock system and its 3 original tables must remain fully operational and untouched, with zero risk of the new engine regressing them.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Isolation between ALI and the existing mock system is structural (separate route, separate tables, separate result type), not a feature flag — there's no shared code path for a bug in ALI to regress the production mock runner through.

### Decision 17 — Weak-skill override guarantees a minimum reserved slot, not just eligibility
**Decision:** When a cooling-down question's competency is in the weak-skill set, the selection algorithm reserves ~20% of the section (minimum 1, capped at what's available) specifically for overridden weak-skill questions, sampled before the general weighted pool fills the rest — rather than merely making them eligible at the same weight as ordinary review.
**Rationale:** Found during implementation testing (Slice 1): giving overridden questions equal weight to ordinary eligible-seen questions made weak-skill remediation *possible* but not *guaranteed* — with enough unseen content competing for the same slots, a weak competency could be crowded out of a mock entirely by chance. This directly failed the "weak competencies are intentionally revisited" success criterion when tested against the synthetic fixture (`lib/ali/selection.ts` test suite, 2026-07-02).
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `selectQuestions()` (`lib/ali/selection.ts`) now has an explicit reserve step (4b) between the override step (4) and the general weighted sample (5). This is a stronger remediation guarantee than originally specified in ADAPTIVE_ASSESSMENT_ENGINE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md v3 §3.2 — the plan described override questions as becoming merely "eligible," this decision makes a minimum inclusion count deterministic.

### Decision 18 — Weak-competency detection is native to ALI, not derived from buildReplayQueue()
**Decision:** `lib/ali/weakness.ts`'s `deriveWeakCompetencies()` computes weak competencies directly from ALI's own `ali_student_question_history.mastery_state` data (any competency with a question currently marked `weak`), rather than reusing the existing `buildReplayQueue()`/`report.skills` signal from `lib/replayEngine.ts`.
**Rationale:** `buildReplayQueue()` operates on the app's coarse `SkillType`, which is uniformly `"verbal-reasoning"` for every VR question (Decision 13) — it has no way to express "weak at codes but strong at analogies." Reusing it for the weak-skill override (Decision 11's target mechanism) would have silently produced only subject-level, not competency-level, remediation — defeating the entire point of tagging competency-level skill on each question. This corrects an inconsistency present in ADAPTIVE_ASSESSMENT_ENGINE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md v3 §4.1, which incorrectly asserted `report.skills` already had the needed granularity.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** ALI computes weakness entirely from its own data — no dependency on the legacy replay engine for this. The legacy `buildReplayQueue()` remains used only for the §0.5.3 bridge (feeding today's Parent Insights/replay UI from the coarse `SkillType` signal), a separate, deliberately-unmigrated concern.

### Decision 19 — Route is `app/mocks/adaptive/gl/page.tsx`, not a dynamic `[pathway]` segment
**Decision:** The Slice 1 route is a static path for GL only, not `app/mocks/adaptive/[pathway]/page.tsx` as sketched in the implementation plan.
**Rationale:** Only GL has adaptive content in Slice 1 (Decision 9); a dynamic segment would need to handle unknown/unimplemented pathway params for zero present benefit. Matches the engineering principle to keep Slice 1 intentionally small and prove the architecture, not complete it — the route can be generalized to a dynamic segment in the slice that actually adds a second adaptive pathway.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** No functional difference to Slice 1's scope. Future slices adding a second adaptive pathway (e.g. ISEB) should revisit this as a deliberate, separate decision about whether to generalize to `[pathway]` at that point.

### Decision 20 — Mastery evidence needs 2 extra history columns beyond the original plan
**Decision:** `ali_student_question_history` (migration 006) includes `last_attempt_correct` and `second_last_attempt_correct` (nullable booleans), in addition to the columns specified in the implementation plan.
**Rationale:** The `weak` mastery_state ("last 2 consecutive attempts both incorrect," Decision 7) needs to know the last 2 outcomes to compute — without storing them, there was no way to derive this signal from the previously-specified columns (`times_seen`/`times_correct` alone can't distinguish "2 wrong in a row" from "1 wrong, 1 right, in either order"). Found and fixed during implementation, before migration 006 was ever applied to any database — no retroactive schema change was needed.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `lib/ali/mastery.ts`'s `applyAttemptOutcome()` reads/writes these two fields directly rather than reconstructing recent history from a log table — keeps the per-question history row small and O(1) to update.

### Decision 21 — Maintain this decision log going forward
**Decision:** `ALI_DECISION_LOG.md` is the permanent architectural history of ALI, appended to (not rewritten) for every future major decision.
**Rationale:** Preserve the reasoning behind ALI's design choices independently of the implementation-plan documents, which get rewritten/superseded across revisions (v1→v2→v3) and would otherwise lose the "why" behind earlier drafts.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Every future ALI architectural decision (e.g. extending to competency-level difficulty, migrating confidence/replay/readiness to read from ALI directly, adding a new subject) gets a new numbered entry here, not a silent change.

### Decision 22 — Phase ALI 1.1: prove Slice 1 before any expansion; observability is console-only
**Decision:** Before any Slice 2 work (new subjects, new adaptive features, new question banks), a dedicated validation phase was run instead. Observability was added as a lightweight, console-only trace (`lib/ali/observability.ts`, `types/ali/observability.ts`) attached to every generated mock section — never persisted to a new table, never exposed to end users.
**Rationale:** "Quality before scale, observability before expansion" — explicit engineering principle for this phase. A console trace was chosen over a persisted table because the ask was for debugging/validation visibility, not a permanent analytics pipeline; adding a new table would itself be exactly the kind of expansion this phase was meant to avoid.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `selectQuestions()` and `buildAdaptiveSection()`'s return types changed from `BankQuestion[]` to `{ questions, trace }` to carry this data out — a mechanical, non-functional refactor of the Slice 1 selection API, not a behavior change (verified via the same pure-function test suite, all still passing).

### Decision 23 — Validation findings are documented, not silently patched
**Decision:** Three real findings surfaced by end-to-end simulation (`ALI_VALIDATION_PROTOCOL.md` §Findings — exam readiness never leaving "not-ready" under ALI-only usage; Daily Mission rarely surfacing VR-specific remediation; small-sample score volatility) were documented as open product questions, not fixed in this phase.
**Rationale:** Findings 1 and 2 are pre-existing characteristics of `lib/parentInsights.ts`/`lib/adaptiveEngine.ts` (session-counting model, cross-subject urgency weighting) that ALI's repeated-single-subject usage pattern exposes but did not cause. Fixing them would mean modifying shared, non-ALI legacy code — explicitly out of this phase's scope ("no new adaptive features," and more importantly, changing shared confidence/mission logic is a cross-cutting product decision, not a Phase 1.1 validation task).
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** These findings block nothing about ALI's own correctness (all 7 validation scenarios passed for ALI's own mechanisms) but should be resolved or explicitly accepted before Slice 2 broadens usage, since they'll only become more visible as more students use ALI-only practice patterns.

### Decision 24 — Root cause of Finding 2 identified: `Math.max` score ratcheting, not just urgency weighting
**Decision:** During Phase ALI 1.2's design review, Finding 2 (Daily Mission rarely surfacing VR-specific remediation) was traced to a more precise root cause than originally stated: `lib/progress.ts`'s `completeLesson()` sets `p.scores[lessonId] = Math.max(existing, newScore)` — a subject's tracked score can only ever ratchet upward, never reflect a genuine later decline. This masks weak-status detection for any subject with repeated attempts (exactly ALI's usage pattern), independent of the urgency-weighting issue Finding 2 originally described.
**Rationale:** This is a pre-existing scoring bug in shared legacy code (`lib/progress.ts`), not an ALI defect and not introduced by ALI — but ALI's repeated-single-subject usage pattern is what makes it visible and consequential. Documented rather than fixed here, consistent with Decision 23's reasoning (shared legacy code changes are a product decision, not a validation-phase task).
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `ALI_LEARNING_MODEL.md` §2.2/§4 proposes fixing this at the input level (ALI-native weak-competency signal bypasses the ratcheted score entirely for urgency purposes) rather than patching `Math.max` itself, since the ratchet also feeds Confidence (§7) and Readiness (§3) — any full fix likely needs to replace "single overwritten peak score" with "recent-N-attempts" as the underlying representation, which is a larger change than this phase's scope.

### Decision 25 — Learning Model Refinement proposed, not implemented
**Decision:** `ALI_LEARNING_MODEL.md` documents proposed evolutions for Readiness (6-dimension weighted score), Daily Missions (ALI-native urgency input + breadth-dominance cap), Parent Insights (competency-level, mastery-framed insight types), Replay (ALI-native data source for competency-covered subjects, existing logic otherwise unchanged), and Confidence (mastery-coverage-weighted formula for ALI-covered subjects). None of this is implemented.
**Rationale:** Explicit instruction — "Update the surrounding platform to take advantage of ALI... No implementation yet." Every proposal follows the same principle established in §1 of that document: prefer ALI-native data where it exists (currently Verbal Reasoning only), leave every non-ALI subject's logic completely unchanged.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Establishes a suggested implementation order (Daily Missions first — smallest, most contained, most directly closes a named finding) but does not approve or schedule any of it. Next step is reviewer decision on which system to implement first, and in what scope.

### Decision 26 — Daily Missions implemented; scope narrowed from the §4.1 proposal per explicit instruction
**Decision:** `urgency()` (`lib/adaptiveEngine.ts`) gained an ALI-native branch (weak-competency flag → 140+ priority; mastery-ratio banding → 60/30/5 as coverage grows) for subjects with attempted ALI data. The legacy formula is preserved byte-for-byte for every other case. `ALI_LEARNING_MODEL.md` §4.1's breadth-dominance cap (reducing "not-started" urgency universally once a breadth floor is met) was **not** implemented — the phase instruction ("for non-ALI subjects, preserve existing behaviour") is stricter than that proposal, which would have changed non-ALI subjects' ranking too.
**Rationale:** Ship the smallest change that satisfies the four literal scope requirements (weak competencies prioritised, mastered competencies deprioritised, untouched subjects can't indefinitely bury a known weakness, balanced overall) without touching anything the instruction said to leave alone.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** The breadth-dominance cap remains available as a future, separately-scoped change if the reviewer wants it — `ALI_MISSION_ENGINE.md` §3 documents the narrowing explicitly so it isn't mistaken for an oversight.

### Decision 27 — Mission reason text bypasses `reasonText()` for ALI-flagged weak competencies, rather than extending it
**Decision:** A new function, `aliReasonText()`, generates mission copy naming specific weak competencies, used only when `urgency()`'s ALI-native branch fired. `reasonText()` itself is completely unmodified.
**Rationale:** `reasonText()`'s existing "name the weak skill" behaviour only fires inside its `subject.status === 'weak'` branch — but a subject can have a real ALI-flagged weak competency while its legacy `status` still reads `"strong"` (the Math.max ratchet, Decision 24, again). Extending `reasonText()`'s existing branch to also fire on `weakSkillLabel` presence regardless of `status` would have changed behaviour for non-ALI subjects too, in the pre-existing (if rare) case where a `SkillType`-level weak skill coexists with a non-weak subject status — violating "preserve existing behaviour for non-ALI subjects." A fully separate function guarantees zero effect on any subject without real ALI weak-competency data.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Found via the validation script itself — an initial implementation attempted the "extend `reasonText()`" approach and its test failed (the weak-competency student's mission reason didn't mention the competency by name, because status was "strong"), which is what surfaced this distinction before it shipped.

### Decision 28 — ALI competency signal bridge added to UserProgress, mirrored locally during a mock (not re-fetched from Supabase)
**Decision:** `app/mocks/adaptive/gl/page.tsx` maintains a local mirror of the VR bank/history (`vrBankRef`/`vrHistoryRef`), updated via the same pure `applyAttemptOutcome()` the real Supabase write uses, and computes the end-of-mock `AliCompetencySignal` from that mirror rather than re-fetching from Supabase after the mock.
**Rationale:** Keeps the mission-bridge write working identically whether or not Supabase is reachable (this sandbox's persistent network gap, Slice 1/Phase 1.1/1.2) — the mirror is updated via the exact same pure function regardless, so there's no behavioural difference between "mirror" and "re-fetch," only a resilience difference.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `deriveCompetencySignal()` (`lib/ali/weakness.ts`) is called once, client-side, at mock completion — no additional Supabase read added to the mock-completion path.

### Decision 29 — "Recently mastered" is a diff against the previous cached signal, not a time window
**Decision:** `AliCompetencySignal` gained `recentlyMasteredCompetencies` — computed as the set-difference between the new mock's `masteredCompetencies` and the previously cached signal's, not "mastered within the last N days."
**Rationale:** A diff against the last recorded state is exact and requires no new bookkeeping (no stored timestamps to compare against, no arbitrary window length to pick and later regret). It also composes naturally with how the signal is already written — once per completed mock — so "recently" precisely means "as of your most recent practice," which is the framing a parent actually cares about.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `deriveCompetencySignal()` gained an optional `previousSignal` parameter (still pure — the caller supplies the previous state, no hidden lookups). A competency that oscillates (mastered → weak → mastered across mocks) will correctly re-appear in "Recently Mastered" each time it's re-achieved, which is the intended behaviour.

### Decision 30 — Percentage-first insights are excluded per-subject, not replaced with new text
**Decision:** For subjects with real ALI data, `buildParentInsights()`'s "strong-subject"/"weak-subject" entries and `buildFocusAreas()`'s weak-subject cards are **omitted entirely** rather than having their text rewritten to a competency-first version inline.
**Rationale:** The competency-first replacement already exists as a structurally separate, richer UI section (`competencySummaries` → the parent page's new "How They're Doing" section, `ALI_PARENT_INTELLIGENCE.md` §1) — duplicating that information as inline text in the generic insights list would be redundant, not "replaced." Omitting the old entry and letting the new section carry that information is the more literal reading of "replace."
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Verified via regression check: for a student with no ALI data on any subject, `buildParentInsights()`/`buildFocusAreas()` output is byte-for-byte identical to the pre-Phase-1.4 formula. For a student with real VR data, the "strong-subject"/"weak-subject" insight simply doesn't mention Verbal Reasoning (or is omitted entirely if VR was the only qualifying subject) — the information isn't lost, it's relocated to the new section.

### Decision 31 — Learning Gain formula is a symmetric weighted delta, not a one-directional "progress" score
**Decision:** `computeLearningGainDelta()` can return a negative number — a mock where previously-mastered competencies turn weak subtracts from the score, it doesn't just add less.
**Rationale:** "Measure improvement over time" only means something if it can also register decline. A purely non-negative "progress" score would silently hide a student who is genuinely regressing, which defeats the stated purpose and would make the eventual Readiness Shadow Model (the flagged future consumer, Decision 24) less trustworthy than the data it's built from.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Weights (mastered +3, newly attempted +1, recovered-from-weak +1, newly weak −2) are illustrative starting values, not calibrated against real usage — same caveat as `ALI_LEARNING_MODEL.md` §3.1's readiness dimension weights. Revisit once real student data exists.

### Decision 32 — Mathematics is the second ALI subject; ALI Version 0.1 (VR-only) is complete
**Decision:** Phase ALI 2.0 expands ALI to Mathematics, added by supplying new metadata (a Mathematics competency taxonomy + question bank content) to the existing architecture — zero ALI module (`lib/ali/*`, `lib/adaptiveMockBuilder.ts`, `lib/adaptiveEngine.ts`, `lib/parentInsights.ts`, all ALI-related types) was modified to support it. The new adaptive Mathematics mock (`app/mocks/adaptive/maths/page.tsx`) is standalone, not tied to a pathway — unlike the GL VR mock (Decision 19), no existing static mock has a pure Mathematics section to fit alongside, so this is a single-section practice session in its own right.
**Rationale:** Explicit instruction — "Reuse the existing ALI architecture. Do not redesign it. Do not duplicate logic... The adaptive engine should operate on Mathematics by supplying different metadata rather than different logic." Also explicit: one subject at a time, no automatic progression to English.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** No new Supabase migration was needed — `subject_type` already included `"maths"` from migration 001 (unlike VR's reasoning subjects, which needed migration 004's additive enum extension). `AliSubject` (`types/ali/questionBank.ts`) already included `"maths"` since it was defined in Slice 1 — confirms the original type design was already forward-compatible for exactly this expansion, not a lucky coincidence but the intended design (architecture §0.5's "consumer-agnostic, subject-agnostic" principle, now tested against a second subject for the first time).

### Decision 33 — Mathematics competency taxonomy required real refinement, same discipline as Decision 13
**Decision:** Reviewing all 20 real `data/maths.ts` questions against the 14 suggested competencies surfaced two genuine gaps — 3 questions testing powers/roots/order-of-operations and 1 testing LCM/factors fit none of the 14 cleanly. Added `maths.powers-roots` and `maths.factors-multiples` as new competencies (16 total), documented in `QUESTION_AUTHORING_STANDARD.md` §11 with the real question IDs that drove each addition.
**Rationale:** Same principle as Decision 13 (VR's `SkillType` granularity gap) — a suggested taxonomy is a starting point, not ground truth; real content must be checked against it before treating it as final, or real questions end up force-fitted into an ill-fitting category, silently degrading the fine-grained competency data ALI depends on.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** `data/maths.ts`'s `MathsQuestion` type has no existing `category` field the way `ReasoningQuestion` did (Decision 13's original finding) — there was no ground truth to read from at all for Mathematics, only the questions' actual content. This makes the review step even more load-bearing here than it was for VR, not less.

### Decision 34 — Multi-topic Mathematics questions get one primary competency by dominant skill, not several
**Decision:** Several real Mathematics questions genuinely test more than one topic (e.g. `mth-003` combines an algebraic setup with a geometry answer). Rather than allow multi-tagging, `QUESTION_AUTHORING_STANDARD.md` §11.3 establishes: tag by the dominant skill that actually determines correctness (the reasoning step, not the surface topic), and treat new questions going forward as required to test one competency cleanly per §2.1's existing writing standard.
**Rationale:** Multi-tagging a question across several competencies would double-count it in mastery-ratio calculations (`lib/ali/weakness.ts`'s `deriveCompetencySignal()`) for every competency it's tagged with, silently inflating attempted-competency counts without a proportional signal increase — a data-integrity problem, not just an authoring-style preference.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** No code change required — `BankQuestion.skill` was already designed as a single string (Decision 13), never a list. This decision is purely an authoring-standard rule, resolving an ambiguity the taxonomy work surfaced rather than a schema gap.

### Decision 35 — Validated end-to-end with zero code changes to any shared ALI module
**Decision:** Phase ALI 2.0's validation script (18 checks, all passing on first run) proved Mathematics gets full Daily Mission prioritisation and Parent Intelligence benefit — including a new cross-subject check (VR + Maths coexisting for one student, each subject's signal correctly isolated from the other's) — without touching `lib/adaptiveEngine.ts`, `lib/parentInsights.ts`, `lib/ali/selection.ts`, `lib/ali/mastery.ts`, `lib/ali/weakness.ts`, `lib/ali/history.ts`, `lib/ali/questionBank.ts`, `lib/ali/observability.ts`, `lib/ali/learningGain.ts`, `lib/adaptiveMockBuilder.ts`, or any ALI type file.
**Rationale:** This is the concrete, falsifiable test of the architecture's central claim (`ADAPTIVE_ASSESSMENT_ENGINE_ARCHITECTURE.md` §0.5: "the mock engine is simply one consumer of this intelligence") — if a second subject had required touching shared logic, that claim would have been wrong. It held.
**Date:** 2026-07-02
**Implications:** Gives real confidence for ALI 2.1/2.2 (English, Vocabulary) that the same zero-shared-code-change pattern should hold again — each new subject's actual cost is a competency taxonomy + question bank + one new standalone route, not architecture work.

### Decision 36 — Learning Unit introduced as a permanent architectural concept
**Decision:** A `learning_unit_id` column is added to `ali_question_bank` (migration 007), and a new module `lib/ali/learningUnit.ts` (`groupQuestionsByLearningUnit()`, `selectLearningUnit()`) is added alongside — not inside — `lib/ali/selection.ts`. A Learning Unit is the schedulable, never-split unit of adaptive selection: atomic subjects (Verbal Reasoning, Mathematics) get `learning_unit_id = id` (a unit of exactly one question, byte-for-byte unchanged behaviour); Reading Comprehension sets `learning_unit_id` to the shared passage id, so every question belonging to one passage resolves to one unit. `selectLearningUnit()` mirrors `selectQuestions()`'s cooldown/weak-skill-override philosophy at unit granularity (previous-mock absolute exclusion, cooldown-by-hardest-constituent-tier, weak-skill override) but picks exactly one unit per mock rather than partitioning many slots — weak-skill override is expressed as the *highest* selection weight in the combined pool, not a reserved-slot floor (Decision 17's mechanism doesn't translate directly to a single pick).
**Rationale:** Explicit instruction: introduce Learning Unit as a permanent concept, and adapt Reading Comprehension's implementation to select a unit first and serve every linked question — without redesigning the existing engine. Writing `selectLearningUnit()` as new code beside `selectQuestions()`, rather than modifying `selectQuestions()` to understand multi-question groups, means VR and Maths keep using the exact function they always have, completely untouched.
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** Future Learning Units (Writing = one writing task, Vocabulary = one word set, per the original architectural requirement) plug into the same `groupQuestionsByLearningUnit()`/`selectLearningUnit()` pair with zero further changes to either function — only new `learning_unit_id` values at import time. `BankQuestion.learningUnitId` is now a required field on every bank row across every subject, including the two existing synthetic fixtures (`vrSyntheticFixture.ts`, `mathsSyntheticFixture.ts`), which were updated to set `learningUnitId: id` per row — a mechanical, behaviour-preserving change, not a redesign.

### Decision 37 — Full-marks-only is the Slice 1 correctness rule for free-text answers
**Decision:** For Reading Comprehension, an attempt counts as "correct" for ALI's binary history/mastery fields (`times_correct`, `last_attempt_correct`, `mastery_state`) only if it earns full marks (`earnedMarks === maxMarks`) under the existing keyword-overlap scoring heuristic (`scoreAnswer()`, duplicated locally in `app/mocks/adaptive/english/page.tsx` from `app/english/[id]/page.tsx`). Partial credit (e.g. 1 of 2 marks) does not count as correct and does not contribute mastery evidence.
**Rationale:** Explicit instruction: keep the implementation simple, document this clearly as an intentional first-pass design. Avoids inventing a partial-credit-aware schema or mastery model before real usage data exists to justify one.
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** No schema change — `applyAttemptOutcome()` (`lib/ali/mastery.ts`) already takes a plain `isCorrect: boolean`, unmodified. This threshold is explicitly a first-pass calibration, not a validated constant (same epistemic status as `ALI_LEARNING_MODEL.md` §3.1's illustrative dimension weights) — if real usage shows it's too strict or too lenient, the threshold is the first thing to revisit, not the schema. Validated via the pure-function script: a 1-of-2-marks attempt does not register as correct or contribute to mastery; two distinct full-marks sessions do reach `mastered`.

### Decision 38 — Reading Comprehension competency approval: only 2 of 10 taxonomy entries are populated
**Decision:** Only `english.inference` and `english.vocabulary-in-context` are approved and used in the question bank / synthetic fixture this phase. The other 8 taxonomy entries from `ENGLISH_COMPETENCY_FRAMEWORK.md` §3 (`english.retrieval`, `english.authors-purpose`, `english.sequencing`, `english.summarising`, `english.prediction`, `english.grammar`, `english.punctuation`, `english.spelling`) remain defined but unused, staying on the roadmap until real content justifies them.
**Rationale:** Explicit instruction: approve only the two competencies with real evidence in the current content; do not invent competencies without evidence.
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** `lib/ali/labels.ts`'s `COMPETENCY_LABELS` dictionary gained exactly these two new entries (`"english.inference": "Inference"`, `"english.vocabulary-in-context": "Vocabulary in Context"`) — a real gap caught before it shipped: without this addition, `competencyLabel()` would have fallen back to the raw code string, leaking `"english.inference"` verbatim into Daily Mission reason text and Parent Insights, violating the "no raw competency code in parent-facing text" bar already established and checked in Phase 1.4. The remaining 8 competencies intentionally have no label entry yet either, since they have no content to be labelled.

### Decision 39 — Reading Comprehension validated end-to-end with zero changes to `lib/ali/selection.ts` or `lib/adaptiveMockBuilder.ts`
**Decision:** Phase ALI 2.1's validation script (23 checks, all passing after fixing test-construction bugs — not engine bugs, see below) proved: different students receive different passages; a weak `english.inference` signal drives 100% of picks to inference-tagged passages across 300 trials with zero vocabulary-in-context picks, and vice versa for a weak vocabulary-in-context signal (fully independent adaptation); the full-marks-only correctness rule behaves as designed; Daily Missions and Parent Insights both correctly surface Reading Comprehension's competencies by name; and a 3-subject coexistence check (VR + Maths + English) showed zero cross-subject signal leakage in any direction.
**Rationale:** The concrete, falsifiable re-test of Decision 35's central claim, now against a subject whose Learning Unit is NOT a single question — if multi-question units had required touching shared engine code, the "consumer-agnostic" claim would have failed under a harder case than Mathematics presented.
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** Three real test-construction bugs were caught and fixed during validation (worth remembering — same value as Slice 1/1.3's iterative debugging, this time in the test harness rather than the code under test): (1) an early version of the cooling-down fixture gave every unit the identical `lastPresentedAtSequence`, causing Step 1's absolute previous-mock exclusion to wipe out every candidate at once — fixed by staggering the stamps; (2) the mastery-threshold simulation used a hardcoded 2 correct sessions for every question, but the fixture's challenge-tier questions need 3 — fixed by reading `q.masteryThreshold` per question; (3) two assertions checked the `strengths` bucket for a competency that had just been mastered with no `previousSignal` supplied, when Decision 29's diff-based design correctly routes a first-time-computed mastery into `recentlyMastered` instead — fixed by asserting the correct bucket. None of the three were bugs in `lib/ali/learningUnit.ts`, `lib/ali/mastery.ts`, or `lib/ali/weakness.ts` themselves.

### Decision 40 — Cross-Subject Recommendations implemented as real code, deliberately not wired into any UI
**Decision:** `lib/ali/recommendations.ts` (`COMPETENCY_RELATIONSHIPS`, `computeCrossSubjectRecommendations()`) implements the model designed in `ALI_CROSS_SUBJECT_INTELLIGENCE.md` — a small, hand-authored relationship graph (6 edges, grounded only in competency codes that actually exist) and a pure function producing conservative, confidence-tiered recommendations. It is not called from `lib/adaptiveEngine.ts`, `lib/parentInsights.ts`, or any route.
**Rationale:** Explicit instruction scoped this phase to "no UI redesign." Matches the exact precedent already established by Learning Gain (Decision 31) — real, tested, internal-only code, safe to ship because it changes nothing a student or parent can see, with a clear, smaller follow-on decision (wiring it in) left for later once real usage exists to validate the confidence thresholds.
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** Two of the graph's 6 edges are structurally inert by design (one points at `numerical-reasoning`, which has no ALI competency data; one is deliberately `weak`-strength and can never fire) — only `strong` shared-mechanism edges (high confidence, requires a mastered source) and non-weak sequential-dependency edges (moderate confidence, requires a weak source) ever produce output. The safety rule ("direct evidence always wins") is enforced structurally, not by priority ordering — a recommendation literally cannot be generated for a competency that already has any direct evidence, checked before any confidence tier is considered. A reclassification was made and flagged, not silently absorbed: `ALI_CROSS_SUBJECT_INTELLIGENCE.md` §1.2 originally sketched Vocabulary/VR as sequential-dependency; implementation reclassified it as shared-mechanism/strong because the wanted behaviour ("strong Vocabulary may support VR") is mastery-driven, matching the shared-mechanism rule, not the weak-source sequential-dependency rule.

### Decision 41 — Learning Profile implemented with 4 of 8 dimensions honestly null, not fabricated
**Decision:** `lib/ali/learningProfile.ts` (`computeLearningProfile()`) computes Logical Reasoning, Verbal Reasoning, and Numerical Confidence as mastery ratios over curated cross-subject competency pools, and Persistence as a coarse resilience signal derived from Learning Gain's cumulative trend for any currently-struggling subject. Learning Consistency, Learning Speed, Confidence vs. Accuracy, and Revision Behaviour are typed as literal `null` (not `number | null`) — they would need raw per-attempt timestamp or response-time data that isn't bridged into `UserProgress` and doesn't exist anywhere in the app yet.
**Rationale:** "The profile must update automatically from existing ALI evidence" was read strictly — a plausible-looking fabricated number for a dimension with zero real backing data would misrepresent what the system actually knows, the same discipline this project has applied to every other honest gap (Decision 38's label gap, `ALI_CROSS_SUBJECT_INTELLIGENCE.md` §2.4's response-time gap). Four real dimensions with real evidence is more valuable and more trustworthy than eight dimensions where half are guesses.
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** `recordAliLearningProfile()` (`lib/progress.ts`) is called from all four adaptive routes' completion handlers, immediately after the existing Learning Gain write — the profile recomputes and re-bridges automatically after every adaptive mock across every subject, satisfying "must update automatically" without needing a new Supabase read or a scheduled job. The parent-friendly interpretation layer (`buildInterpretation()`) is built and tested but stored internally only, not rendered anywhere — same internal-only status as the signal it's derived from and as Decision 40's recommendations.

### Decision 42 — Vocabulary is the second Learning Unit subject; "Word Set" interpreted as one word's full item set
**Decision:** Vocabulary's Learning Unit (`VOCABULARY_COMPETENCY_FRAMEWORK.md` §6) is one word plus every MCQ item generated from it (synonym/antonym/in-context), not a themed batch of several distinct words. `learning_unit_id` = the word's own id, shared across its items — reusing `lib/ali/learningUnit.ts` with zero code changes, the concrete re-test of Decision 36's forward-compatibility claim.
**Rationale:** "Word Set" was ambiguous between "a batch of several words" and "the set of facets tested about one word" — the real existing app has no mechanic that batches multiple distinct words into one indivisible unit, but does naturally support multiple distinct testable facets per word (the existing flashcard already shows synonyms, antonyms, and an example sentence together for one word). The interpretation that matches real content, not the interpretation that sounds more literally like "a set of words," was chosen — flagged explicitly rather than picked silently.
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** Zero new migration was needed for Vocabulary at all (unlike English's `open-response` question_type addition, itself already schema-free) — `subject_type` already had `'vocabulary'` from migration 001, `question_type` stays `'multiple-choice'`, and `learning_unit_id` (migration 007) is already subject-agnostic. This is the lightest schema footprint of any ALI subject added so far, a real data point supporting the "consumer-agnostic architecture" claim, not just an assertion.

### Decision 43 — Vocabulary competency taxonomy: 3 of 10 suggested competencies grounded, 7 blocked on more than content
**Decision:** Only `vocabulary.synonyms`, `vocabulary.antonyms`, `vocabulary.in-context` are approved — each has a direct, populated field on the real `VocabWord` type (`synonyms[]`, `antonyms[]`, `exampleSentence`). The other 7 suggested competencies (multiple meanings, prefixes, suffixes, root words, homophones, idioms, word families) have zero grounding — reviewed against all 12 real words and found absent not just as content but as a schema capability: `VocabWord` has no field that could represent a word's morphology or multiple senses at all.
**Rationale:** Same discipline as Decisions 13/33/38 — check the suggested list against real content before adopting it. This case is a harder version of the pattern: English's 8 unpopulated competencies (Decision 38) only needed new content in an existing format; Vocabulary's 7 needed a schema change before a tagging pass would even be possible, a real distinction worth naming rather than collapsing both into "not done yet."
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** `lib/ali/labels.ts` gained exactly the 3 approved competency labels — the other 7 have no label entry, matching the established "no label for content that doesn't exist yet" precedent. Populating any of the 7 is now explicitly a two-step prerequisite (schema change, then content) in `ALI_VERSION.md`'s known gaps, not a one-step "write more words" task.

### Decision 44 — ALI Foundation Version 1.0 declared complete; production activation is the sole remaining gate
**Decision:** With Vocabulary (2.2), Cross-Subject Recommendations, Learning Profiles, and this operations manual complete, ALI's architecture is declared feature-complete for its first foundation version. No further subject or feature expansion is scheduled until migrations are applied, all four subjects are hand-tagged and seeded, and live validation is run (`ALI_OPERATIONS_MANUAL.md` §11, `ALI_VERSION.md` Roadmap).
**Rationale:** Explicit instruction: complete the remaining foundational components, then pause. Four subjects proving the "reuse, don't redesign" claim four times over (Decisions 35, 39, and this phase's), plus two internal-only extensions (recommendations, learning profiles) built to the same real-code-but-unexposed standard as Learning Gain, plus a permanent operations handbook, constitute a coherent, closeable unit of work rather than an open-ended list of features.
**Date:** 2026-07-03
**Implications:** The next real milestone for ALI is operational, not architectural — `ALI_PRODUCTION_ACTIVATION_CHECKLIST.md` then `ALI_HAND_TAGGING_WORKFLOW.md` then `ALI_SEEDING_PLAN.md` then `ALI_LIVE_VALIDATION_PROTOCOL.md`, in that order, for each of the four subjects, whenever a human with real Supabase Dashboard access is ready to execute it. No further design documents should be produced for ALI unless a genuine new requirement or blocker emerges.

### Decision 45 — CSSE Vocabulary remains evidence under RC-03; no separate Vocabulary competency or QT-VOC taxonomy
**Decision:** Founder-approved (Educational Increment 007A). Direct reading of the registered Level-A CSSE English evidence (2022/2023 Main Test papers, Founder-Accepted) found no standalone vocabulary section or task in either year — every vocabulary demand present (2023: "explain what you think the verb 'to bask' means"; 2022: a 5-word synonym battery tied to specific line numbers) is embedded inside Reading Comprehension, always anchored to a word-in-context, never decontextualised recall. Vocabulary is therefore evidence under the existing `RC-03` competency ("Word/Phrase Meaning-in-Context Explanation"), which already exists in Assessment Brain with two Question Types already mapped to it (`QT-RC-03`, `QT-RC-04`). No new `QT-VOC-xx` family, and no Assessment Brain amendment, is approved.
**Rationale:** Decision 43 already established the discipline of checking a suggested competency list against real grounding before adopting it (3 of 10 approved, 7 blocked for lacking any schema/content backing). This is the same discipline applied one level up: the *product feature* "Vocabulary" existing (flashcards, `app/vocabulary/page.tsx`) does not by itself justify a competency existing for it in the CSSE-aligned Assessment Brain — the exam evidence, not product preference, decides. The existing standalone Vocabulary product experience (decontextualised word/definition/synonym flashcards) is explicitly **not** approved as CSSE-aligned merely because it exists; its future role must be reconciled separately against pathway evidence, not assumed from this decision.
**Date:** 2026-08-13
**Implications:** Future English content authoring that wants to build vocabulary-in-context supply should author under `QT-RC-03`/`QT-RC-04`, tied to a real passage, not build a new competency or standalone question format. This closes, until genuinely new evidence emerges, the open question `lib/learningEngine/practiceContent.ts`'s own code comment raised ("Assessment Brain V1 defines no Vocabulary competency... Formally BLOCKED") — the blocker is resolved by attaching Vocabulary to RC-03, not by amending Assessment Brain.

### Decision 46 — WC-02 approved at architecture level: one new Question Type, same submission as WC-01, gated on scoring capability
**Decision:** Founder-approved in principle (Educational Increment 007A). Direct reading of the registered CSSE Continuous Writing Sample Mark Scheme (Level A, Founder-Accepted) confirms a real, single, 4-band, 5-dimension rubric (Ideas, Vocabulary incl. spelling, Grammar, Structure, Punctuation) applied to one writing task — WC-02 ("Multi-Dimensional Writing Quality") is not an invented or unsupported competency, it corresponds directly to this rubric. One new Question Type is approved at architecture level, attached to the *same* learner submission WC-01 already produces — not a second writing task. Formal WC-02 evidence must **not** be activated until the scoring mechanism is capable of producing valid evidence from the CSSE-supported rubric (see Decision 47) — the Question Type existing in the taxonomy does not itself authorise generating evidence from today's non-conformant scorer.
**Rationale:** Matches this log's established evidence-before-architecture discipline (Decisions 38, 43, 45): WC-02 was previously an orphaned taxonomy entry with zero mapped Question Types, easy to mistake for an invented placeholder. Reading the actual rubric shows it is real and well-grounded; the correct fix is a scoring-engine rebuild feeding a new Question Type, not a taxonomy amendment or a second writing prompt burdening the learner twice for one skill.
**Date:** 2026-08-13
**Implications:** No `QT-WC-02` content should be authored, and no WC-02 evidence should be recorded, until Decision 47's defect is remediated. The eventual Question Type reuses the 4 existing writing prompts (`data/writing.ts`) — no new prompt authoring is implied by this decision.

### Decision 47 — Continuous Writing's 0-100 scoring model confirmed as a production educational defect
**Decision:** Founder-confirmed (Educational Increment 007A). The live `/api/writing-feedback` endpoint's free-floating 0-100 `overallScore` is independently verified, by direct reading of the official CSSE Continuous Writing Sample Mark Scheme, to be structurally unrelated to the real assessment model — CSSE uses a 4-band, 5-dimension *descriptive* rubric with no numeric total anywhere, not a different set of numeric bands. This is a confirmed defect, not a disclosed simplification: its replacement (genuine per-dimension band classification against the real rubric) is required before Continuous Writing can be considered educationally complete or WC-02 evidence can be activated (Decision 46).
**Rationale:** `CONTINUOUS_WRITING_EXCELLENCE_MODEL_V1.md`'s already-approved TEACH/PRACTICE/FEEDBACK/EVIDENCE/FORMAL-ASSESSMENT design had already named this gap; this decision is the independent, primary-source confirmation that closes any doubt about whether the gap is real, so a later session cannot reopen "is this actually a defect" without new evidence — only "when do we fix it" remains open.
**Date:** 2026-08-13
**Implications:** Not rebuilt in Educational Increment 007A — the endpoint's current behaviour, while evidence-misaligned, self-discloses to learners as non-authoritative feedback already, so no immediate correctness fix was judged necessary at this time. The rebuild is a distinct, separately-scoped future increment; `CONTINUOUS_WRITING_EXCELLENCE_MODEL_V1.md` remains the design to implement against, not to be redesigned.

### Decision 48 — Anon-key `200` + `[]` is never proof an RLS-protected table is empty
**Decision:** A PostgREST response of `200` with an empty array (even with an authoritative-looking `count=exact` / `content-range: */0` header) must never be reported as "the table is empty" when the table's RLS state is unknown or deliberately restrictive. It is structurally indistinguishable, via an anon key alone, from "RLS is silently filtering every row from this role." The only way to resolve the ambiguity is an authoritative database-level check — the Founder's own authenticated Supabase Table Editor session, or a service-role key (neither of which exists in this repo's scripts by design). Verification tooling must report the two states separately: `ANON/API VISIBLE ROWS: N` and `AUTHORITATIVE DATABASE STATE: <confirmed empty | confirmed N rows | not established through this path>` — never collapse them into one claim.
**Rationale:** This exact mistake was made twice in real production verification during Educational Increment 006B/007A/007B (`ali_family_review` across migrations 038/041/042, then `ali_passage_bank` across migrations 044/045) — both times an anon-key `200`+`[]` was initially reported as "0 rows in the database," and both times the Founder's own Table Editor evidence proved rows existed all along. The second occurrence happened even after the first had already been named as a lesson, which is exactly why this decision exists as a standing, quotable entry rather than a one-off closure-report footnote: a future session re-deriving this from first principles, under time pressure, is the realistic failure mode this decision is written to prevent.
**Date:** 2026-08-13
**Implications:** `scripts/check-family-review-raw.mjs` and `scripts/wave3-family-review-check.mjs` already carry this distinction in their own output and comments (Educational Increment 006B). Any future script querying a table that might be RLS-protected — `ali_passage_bank`, `ali_family_review`, or any new internal-governance table — must follow the same pattern from the start, not retrofit it after a false "empty" claim is already made. A quick, genuinely reliable non-Table-Editor alternative check (a `404` control against a deliberately nonexistent table name proves the target table's `200`+`[]` isn't itself a 404-masquerading-as-empty artefact) narrows the ambiguity somewhat but does not resolve it — RLS-filtered and genuinely-empty still look identical at the HTTP level even after that control.

### Decision 49 — Mock Weekend architecture: Practice is generally available, Mock is a controlled assessment event (design decision only, not implemented)
**Decision:** Recorded verbatim from the Founder's Educational Increment 007D directive as the standing future architecture for Mock, not implemented in 007D or any prior increment. Practice remains generally available to learners at any time (the existing `/learning-intelligence/practice` pathway). Mock is architecturally distinct: a controlled assessment event, not a mode a learner can enter freely.
- **Cadence:** approximately every 2 weeks, preferably as a scheduled weekend event ("Mock Weekend"), not continuously available.
- **Learner experience during a sitting:** countdown/readiness screen before start; exam-style timing; no hints; no teaching content surfaced during the sitting (the MODEL/Guided Practice/exam-strategy infrastructure built in 007B/007C must not leak into Mock); controlled submission (cannot be silently abandoned/resumed like Practice); a result and diagnostic report at the end; the next learning cycle is generated from that result, closing the loop back into Practice.
- **Parent control:** a parent can activate an additional Mock sitting outside the normal cadence when they judge it appropriate; the learner cannot self-unlock unlimited Mocks; a parent-triggered Mock must be distinguishable in the data model from a scheduled Mock Weekend (not silently merged into the same category).
- **Mock content:** drawn from a separate sealed reserve, not the live Practice pool — unseen item/family/passage protection is required (a learner must not be able to encounter in Mock something they were drilled on in Practice, defeating the point of an assessment). No ordinary Practice content may leak into Mock. Copyright-safe, Angel-original content only, unless external rights are separately confirmed.
**Rationale:** Makes explicit, before any Mock implementation work begins, that Mock is not "Practice with a timer" — it is a structurally separate governed pathway with its own content reserve, its own access control, and its own reporting loop. Recording this now prevents a future increment from ad hoc reinventing Mock's shape mid-implementation, and gives the existing Mock firewall discipline (Mock Eligible held at 0 throughout 006B/007A/007B/007C/007D) a concrete target architecture to eventually unlock into, rather than an indefinite freeze with no destination.
**Date:** 2026-08-13
**Implications:** Not implemented in 007D. No `ali_question_bank` row may be marked `mock_eligible` and no Mock-sitting UI may be built until a dedicated Mock implementation increment is separately authorised, at which point this decision is the design to build against, not to be rederived. The sealed-reserve/unseen-protection requirement in particular has implications for how the review-and-promotion pipeline (007D) should eventually be extended: promoting a family to `practice_eligible` is not the same decision as promoting a (likely disjoint) subset to a future `mock_eligible`-equivalent state, and the two must not be conflated when that increment is scoped.

### Decision 50 — Founder authorised as the first Educational Reviewer, for the 007F pilot only, roles kept conceptually separate
**Decision:** The Founder is a legitimate Educational Reviewer (Operating Model role C, `ANGEL_EDUCATIONAL_REVIEW_OPERATING_MODEL_V1.md` §1/§2) for the 007F pilot batch, because the qualification standard's bar is independence from authorship plus demonstrable relevant competence — the Founder did not author the reviewed content and has real 11+ preparation experience, which satisfies §2 on its own terms. This is explicitly a pilot authorisation, not a standing policy that the Founder must personally review all future Angel content, and not a precedent that Founder Approver sign-off (role D) can substitute for Educational Reviewer sign-off (role C) in general.
**Rationale:** The two roles are recorded separately in every review row regardless of who holds them: when the Founder reviews educational content through `/admin-beta/review`, the `reviewer` field names them acting as an Educational Reviewer, with qualification basis "Founder, 11+ preparation experience, programme owner" (or the Founder's own more precise wording); this is never to be read as, or silently merged with, a Founder Approver programme-level decision. The distinction matters because the whole point of the review gate (migration 034, 2021) was that content is never self-certified by whoever has authority over it generally — the Founder reviewing as a real, non-authoring human is compatible with that; a Founder Approver decree that content is "approved" without going through the actual review criteria would not be.
**Date:** 2026-08-13
**Implications:** Every `ali_family_review` row the Founder submits through the pilot must carry their real identity and this qualification basis, never `'UNASSIGNED'` and never a role label alone. Longer term (Educational Increment 007F §11, Review Throughput Plan), additional qualified independent reviewers should be brought in per subject so the Founder is not the permanent review bottleneck — this decision authorises the pilot, not the long-run operating pattern.

### Decision 51 — First Educational Review Pilot closed: 7 of 7 pilot targets decided `approved`, reviewer Ayobami Lawal
**Decision:** The 007E/007F pilot batch is reconciled as closed with a clean result, on the authority of the Founder's own authenticated inspection of `public.ali_family_review` in the Supabase Table Editor (Level 1 evidence — this table has been RLS-opaque to every anon-key/script path used in this repository throughout 007D-007G by design, migration 054, so no anon-key or automated re-derivation of this fact exists or is expected to). The 7 pilot targets — `wave2-fam-multiselect`, `wave1-fam-sequencing`, `wave1-fam-quote-explain`, `wave1-fam-two-character`, `wave1-fam-vocab-explain`, `wave2-eng-surprise` (passage), `mr02-compare` — each carry a completed review row: reviewer "Ayobami Lawal", decision `approved`, visible criteria `TRUE`. None carry `approved_with_amendment`, `requires_revalidation`, or `rejected`. The original `UNASSIGNED`/`pending_independent_review` placeholder rows for all 7 remain present and untouched (confirmed both by the Founder's direct observation and independently by this repository's own `submitReview()` code path, which only ever `INSERT`s a new row and has no `UPDATE`/`DELETE` capability against this table by RLS design).
**Rationale:** Closes 007F's reconciliation, which had been explicitly blocked pending exactly this evidence (007F's own report stated Parts 1/2/3/8 could not be completed without either the Founder's Table Editor evidence or their direct statement of each target's decision). Recorded here, rather than only in a report, so a future session does not need to re-derive or re-request this fact.
**Date:** 2026-08-13
**Implications:** `ANGEL_007D_REVIEW_BACKLOG_V1.md` and `ANGEL_007E_PILOT_REVIEW_PACK_V1.md` are updated to mark these 7 targets' review status as `approved` rather than `pending_independent_review`. This decision is the input to Educational Increment 007G's activation manifest (migration 055) — it authorises computing which content is eligible for the *controlled activation process* (Operating Model §5), not an automatic promotion; eligibility_status still only changes when the Founder applies a scoped, idempotent migration naming exact IDs.

### Decision 52 — Real learner-path verification is a standing release gate: automated tests alone are not sufficient evidence of educational production readiness
**Decision:** For every material educational behaviour change or newly activated content batch, Angel requires representative, risk-based verification through the real learner pathway (the actual Practice UI a learner or reviewer uses), in addition to and never instead of automated tests. Angel's release standard now distinguishes two categories of evidence:
- **AUTOMATED VERIFICATION** — unit/integration tests, `tsc --noEmit`, Copy Quality Guard, production build, and direct database re-derivation of stored content (e.g. independently recomputing a Mathematics answer from its own question parameters, not just checking it's present). Necessary, and often sufficient to catch algorithmic bugs.
- **REAL LEARNER-PATH VERIFICATION** — walking the actual UI a learner or reviewer will use, for a representative sample including boundary cases. This is the only category that has ever caught an instruction/UI/scoring-contract mismatch in this project's history, because those defects are invisible to a test written against the same (incorrect) assumption the UI code makes.
This is a **risk-based representative** requirement, not a mandate to manually test every sibling question in every family — a family's representative and boundary instances, walked once through the real pathway, stands in for its full sibling set the same way `ali_family_review`'s own review unit already works at the family level (migration 034).
**Rationale:** Directly evidenced twice now, not hypothetically. (1) Educational Increment 007G: 183 passing automated tests coexisted with a live production defect in the sequencing family's Guided Practice scoring contract — a fully correct guided answer was marked wrong — found only because a real learner's live attempt on `w1-kitemaker-06` surfaced it, not because a test caught it (`ANGEL_007D_REVIEW_BACKLOG_V1.md`-era work; [[project_angel11plus_007dg_review_governance_activation]] and this log's own governance history). (2) Educational Increment 007H, this same session: preparing Batch 2's review pack surfaced that `wave1-fam-emotion-cause`'s Guided Practice scaffold (`staged-quotation`, reading `prompt.quotationRequired`) was mapped against a family whose content has never populated that field, since the family is actually scored via `TIER5_NAMED_COMPONENT_PLUS_EXPLANATION` against `prompt.acceptedAnswers`. Every automated check available (197/197 tests, `tsc`, Copy Quality Guard, production build) was clean before this was found, because none of them exercised the live Practice page's actual button-click behaviour against this specific family's actual data shape — the same class of gap as 007G's. Both defects were structurally invisible to schema-shape and unit-level testing and would only ever surface through the real pathway a learner or reviewer actually uses.
**Date:** 2026-08-14
**Implications:** Before any future controlled activation, `eligibility_status` promotion, or newly-built teaching/scoring mechanic is presented as production-ready, the closing report must state explicitly what was verified through the real learner/reviewer pathway, not only what passed automated checks. A report that states test/build/lint counts alone, without a real-pathway verification statement, does not meet this bar. This does not retroactively invalidate any prior activation; it governs decisions from Educational Increment 007H onward.

### Decision 55 — Mathematics unit-answer validation defect found and corrected: stored-answer formatting must never become an unstated marking requirement
**Decision:** `checkMathsAnswer()` (`lib/learningEngine/practiceContent.ts`) stripped `£`/`$`/`,`/`°` from a stored answer before comparing it numerically to a learner's answer, but never stripped letter-based measurement units (`m`, `kg`, `l`, `g`...). For any question whose canonical answer is a number with a letter unit suffix and no semicolon-delimited bare-number alternative, a learner who computed the mathematically correct value and typed only the number, the natural response for most children, was marked wrong. Confirmed against the real function, not by inference, and confirmed **already live**: `mr01-measurement-conversion` (4 questions, `practice_eligible` since migration 057/Batch 3) plus `mr03-mixed-perimeter` and `mr04-far-recipe` (6 questions, provisional, both 007K Batch 4 candidates) — 10 questions total, precisely determined by scanning the full Mathematics bank rather than assumed. Fixed by adding a controlled unit-aware comparison path (`parseNumberWithUnit()`, same file): accepts a bare number, a number with the correct unit, or a number with the correct unit separated by a space, against the actual unit vocabulary found in the bank (mm/cm/m/km, mg/g/kg, ml/l) — activating only when the canonical answer's non-numeric tail is a recognised unit, so categorical answers (`Equilateral`, `A`, `True`) and the existing £/$/,/°/semicolon-alternate paths are provably untouched (21 new direct tests plus a 166-check, 146-row full-bank self-consistency and regression sweep, all passing). A present-but-*wrong* unit (e.g. `4.25kg` for a length question) is deliberately still rejected — every family authored this way already states the target unit in the question itself, so unit choice is not the assessed skill, but a mismatched unit is a genuine comprehension error, not incidental formatting. No database content was changed; the stored answers were always educationally correct, only the comparison logic was wrong.
**Rationale:** The general lesson, worth recording independently of this specific fix: stored-answer formatting must never accidentally become an educational marking requirement unless presentation or unit expression is explicitly part of the skill being assessed. This is the same category of error as 007H's `wave1-fam-emotion-cause` Guided Practice defect (a mechanism silently depending on a field/format the content didn't actually provide) — both were structurally invisible to schema-shape checks and were only found by directly tracing what a real learner's exact input would do, reinforcing Decision 52's standing real-learner-path verification requirement.
**Date:** 2026-08-14
**Implications:** `app/maths/page.tsx` (legacy, separate `data/maths.ts` content pool) and `app/mocks/adaptive/maths/page.tsx` (Mock) carry their own independent copies of the same `normalizeNumeric` pattern by this project's established "isolation via separate route" convention — both are disclosed as a known, pre-existing, architecturally-separate duplication of the same category of issue, deliberately not touched by this fix (out of scope: one is not the ALI Practice pathway, the other is Mock). If either is ever revisited, this decision and its fix are the reference to check against, not to be silently re-derived. Any future Mathematics family whose canonical answer is a number with a unit MUST be checked against this same acceptance rule before being proposed for review.
