### Decision 89 — Programme Increment 008D, Post-Migration Production Verification: migration 070 confirmed installed (tables, functions, zero real content); a genuine, precisely-bounded least-privilege finding disclosed (anon can invoke the functions but cannot get past identity derivation) with a generated hardening fix; the one item requiring a fixture (live redaction proof) fully specified and left pending Founder approval — PASS WITH FINDINGS, not yet genuinely CLOSED

**Decision:** Following the Founder's manual application of migration 070 ("Success. No rows returned" — not accepted as sufficient evidence alone), independent verification was performed without inserting any fixture, per the directive's own explicit instruction.

**Confirmed clean, without any fixture**: all 3 tables exist (anon-key probe returns `200`, not a schema error); all 5 functions exist (a bare anon-key RPC call to each reached a real `P0001` application exception, never a "function not found" error); all 3 tables contain exactly 0 rows — no real form, attempt, or answer exists; production content counts exact match (TOTAL 312/PE 295/Mock Eligible 0); `ali_passage_bank` unaffected; Practice content confirmed fully readable; migration 069's own `mock_eligible` protection unaffected; full suite 561/561, TypeScript clean, Copy Quality Guard PASS, build succeeds.

**A genuine finding, precisely bounded, not glossed over**: a bare, unauthenticated anon-key call to each of the 5 functions was not rejected at the Postgres permission layer — it executed and reached real application logic, contradicting migration 070's own stated "authenticated only, never anon" intent (likely a Supabase project-level default-privilege grant to `anon` that `revoke all ... from public` alone does not remove). **Practical impact precisely bounded**: `auth.uid()` is `NULL` for a bare anon-key request, so every function's own identity-derivation step matches no profile and fails before reaching any content or mutation — no anon caller can retrieve a redacted question, submit an answer, or mutate any attempt. This is a least-privilege gap, not a content-exposure or state-mutation defect. A hardening migration (`071_mock_attempt_functions_revoke_anon.sql`, 3 new structural tests, all passing) was generated to close it explicitly — **not applied**, pending the Founder's own catalog query confirming the exact root cause.

**The one item genuinely requiring a fixture, fully specified per the directive's own required format, not inserted**: proving `mock_get_question`'s redaction live (not just structurally) requires at least one `ali_mock_form` row, since attempt creation fails immediately without one. Exact insert SQL, every object it creates (one form row; if exercised, one throwaway anonymous auth identity + one attempt/answer pair, never a real learner's own data), exact cleanup SQL, and proof cleanup returns production to exactly 0 rows across all three tables (with `ali_question_bank` never written to at any point) were all recorded in the governance doc. **Founder approval is required before this fixture is inserted — not inserted this session.**

**Verification:** Full suite 561/561 (558 baseline + 3 new). TypeScript clean. Copy Quality Guard PASS (0 violations, 242 files). Production build succeeds.

**Production counts, re-queried live: TOTAL 312, Practice Eligible 295, Mathematics PE 175, English PE 120, Provisional 17, Mock Eligible 0 — unchanged, zero discrepancy.**

**What this decision does NOT claim:** it does not claim 008D is genuinely, fully closed (two items remain open: the anon-execute root-cause confirmation, and the fixture-based live redaction proof); it does not claim migration 071 has been applied; it does not claim any fixture was inserted; it does not claim any real Mock content, form, or attempt exists.

**Date:** 2026-08-17

**Rationale:** Distinguishing "the core security property (redaction, ownership) is not broken" from "the stated design (authenticated-only execute grants) was fully achieved" is the same precision this project's own Evidence Hierarchy discipline demands elsewhere — a defense-in-depth gap is a real, disclosable finding even when the practical exploit surface is empty, and naming it precisely (rather than either overstating it as a breach or ignoring it as immaterial) is what lets the Founder make an informed, proportionate decision about the hardening fix.

**Implications:** 008D's engine is confirmed installed and structurally sound; one small, generated, ready-to-apply hardening fix and one fully-specified, Founder-gated fixture stand between this and genuine closure. No further increment begins automatically; the next controlled programme activity after 008D's own closure is the Mock Visual and Product Experience Standard, separately authorised, not begun here.

### Decision 90 — Programme Increment 008D, Security Hardening Reconciliation: Founder's own authenticated catalogue evidence proved a SECOND finding beyond Decision 89's own — `ali_mock_form`'s SELECT policy exposed the sealed question manifest itself to anon/authenticated; migration 071 revised in place (never applied) to close both findings together; `ali_mock_form` becomes ADMIN/SERVER ONLY

**Decision:** The Founder's Level 1 catalogue evidence confirmed Decision 89's Finding A exactly (`EXECUTE` granted to `anon, authenticated, postgres, service_role` on all 5 functions) and surfaced a second, genuine, previously-undisclosed finding: `ali_mock_form_select_all` grants `anon, authenticated` unconditional SELECT (`qual = true`) on `ali_mock_form`, whose `question_manifest` column is the ordered array of real question IDs and section mapping — the sealed form structure itself, not merely inert metadata as migration 070's own original design comment (mistakenly) reasoned.

**`ali_mock_form` schema classified column-by-column**: `id`/`specification_version`/`attempt_type`/`active`/`created_at` are PUBLIC-SAFE; `question_manifest` is **SEALED UNTIL ATTEMPT**. This was a genuine design error in 008D's own first pass — disclosed plainly as such, not minimised — correctly caught by the Founder's own review before migration 071 could be treated as sufficient.

**Migration 071, classified INCOMPLETE against its original scope, revised in place** (never applied, so editing it directly — rather than issuing a new migration number — follows this repository's own established practice for an unapplied migration, the same discipline already used for 007X's migration 067). The revised migration now: revokes `anon` execute on all 5 functions (Finding A, unchanged); **drops `ali_mock_form_select_all` entirely**, with no replacement SELECT policy for `anon` or ordinary `authenticated` (Finding B); leaves `authenticated` execute on the 5 functions, `ali_mock_form_admin_write`, and `ali_mock_attempt`/`ali_mock_attempt_answer`'s own ownership policies all untouched.

**Final form access contract**: `ali_mock_form` becomes ADMIN/SERVER ONLY, mirroring `ali_passage_bank`'s own established precedent exactly (migration 054) — no learner-safe projection was invented, since none is currently needed (`attempt_type` already flows through `mock_start_attempt`'s own return value; question content already flows through `mock_get_question`'s own redacted, one-question-at-a-time projection). A future learner-facing form title/duration display, if ever wanted, should extend an attempt function's own return value, not a direct table grant — named for 008E/008F, not built.

**Tests revised**: 8 tests (from the original 3) covering all 14 items of the directive's own required list, either structurally or via live production checks.

**Verification:** Full suite 566/566 (558 baseline + 8 revised tests). TypeScript clean. Copy Quality Guard PASS (0 violations, 242 files). Production build succeeds.

**Production counts, re-queried live: TOTAL 312, Practice Eligible 295, Mathematics PE 175, English PE 120, Provisional 17, Mock Eligible 0 — unchanged, zero discrepancy.**

**The one fixture-gated item (live redaction proof) remains necessary and unchanged in its exact, fully-specified plan** (insert SQL, every object created, real-learner impact assessment, cleanup SQL, post-cleanup zero-row verification) — now explicitly sequenced after migration 071's own application. **Not inserted this session. Founder approval required.**

**What this decision does NOT claim:** it does not claim migration 071 has been applied; it does not claim 008D is genuinely closed; it does not claim any fixture was inserted; it does not claim any real Mock content, form, or attempt exists; it does not claim a learner-safe form projection was built (deliberately not needed yet).

**Date:** 2026-08-17

**Rationale:** A second real finding surfacing during the SAME reconciliation the first one triggered is exactly why this project treats Founder-supplied Level 1 catalogue evidence as authoritative over a generator's own structural self-testing — the original migration's own tests correctly proved what the SQL text said, but could not catch a design reasoning error (treating question-ID structure as non-sealed) that only became visible once the real, installed policy was inspected against the real column contents by someone reviewing the actual security implication, not just the SQL syntax.

**Implications:** Both findings from the Founder's own review are now addressed in one coherent, revised, not-yet-applied migration, closing the gap between "structurally correct" and "correctly scoped" before any real Mock content is ever placed behind this firewall. No further increment begins automatically; the next controlled programme activity after 008D's eventual closure remains the separately-authorised Mock Visual and Product Experience Standard, not begun here.

### Decision 91 — Programme Increment 008D, Final Closure Reconciliation: Founder-supplied Level 1 evidence confirms migration 071 applied, live redaction proof exercised via a governed fixture, fixture and diagnostic identity fully removed; 008D genuinely CLOSED

**Decision:** The Founder reported migration 071 applied via Supabase Dashboard (not this session's own action) and supplied catalogue-derived evidence that: `anon` has zero `EXECUTE` grants on all 5 Mock attempt functions; `authenticated`, `postgres`, and `service_role` retain `EXECUTE`; `ali_mock_form_select_all` no longer exists; `ali_mock_form`'s only remaining policy is `ali_mock_form_admin_write` (`authenticated`, `ALL`, `is_current_user_admin()`); RLS remains enabled on all three Mock tables. This is an exact match to migration 071's own intended effect (Decision 90).

**Live redaction proof exercised.** The Founder created a governed fixture, exercised the real RPC pathway (`mock_create_attempt` → `mock_start_attempt` → `mock_get_question`), and supplied the literal returned JSON payload:
```
{"marks":1,"skill":"QT-MR-02","subject":"maths","question":"▢ × 7 = 84. What number replaces the box?","questionId":"mr01-mop-01","contentDifficulty":"medium"}
```
The ordinary source question row also contains `id`, `answer`, and `workingSteps`. Neither field is present in the returned payload — recursive inspection reported zero protected fields in the learner-facing Mock payload. This is consistent with, and now a live confirmation of, this repository's own structural test: *"mock_get_question only ever returns the explicit, hand-picked safe field set — never select * or a superset"* (re-run clean this session, see below).

**Boundary behaviour reported and consistent with design:** a question outside the assigned form manifest was rejected; `mock_submit_answer` returned no correctness signal; `mock_submit_attempt` locked the attempt; the locked attempt refused further `mock_get_question`/`mock_submit_answer` calls; an ordinary authenticated learner could not enumerate `ali_mock_form` or read `question_manifest` directly. All consistent with the four-check enforcement (ownership, in-progress status, not expired, question-in-manifest) already proved structurally in Decision 88/90's own test suite.

**Fixture and diagnostic identity fully removed.** The Founder reported, and independently verified before deletion, that every dependent table (`ali_student_question_history`, `ali_student_adaptive_state`, `ali_durable_mastery`, `ali_educational_audit`, `ali_operational_events`, `ali_family_focus_selection`, `user_stats`, `lesson_progress`, `ali_mock_attempt`, `feedback_submissions`, `bug_reports`, `feature_requests`, `beta_family_applications`, `testimonials`) held zero rows tied to the diagnostic identity before the `auth.users` row was deleted via Supabase Dashboard → Authentication → Users (not raw SQL, avoiding orphaned dependent rows). Post-deletion: `auth.users` = 0, `profiles` = 0 for that identity. `ali_mock_form` / `ali_mock_attempt` / `ali_mock_attempt_answer` all reported back to 0 rows.

**Production counts reported unchanged:** TOTAL 312, Practice Eligible 295, Mathematics PE 175, English PE 120, Provisional 17, Mock Eligible 0 — matching Decision 90's own last-confirmed figures exactly, zero discrepancy reported.

**Independently re-executed this session** (this environment has no Supabase service-role credential, `psql`, or Supabase CLI — production/security claims above rest on Founder-supplied Level 1 evidence, exactly as Decisions 86/87/89/90 already established as this project's practice, not a new gap introduced this session): full test suite **566/566 pass** (unchanged count from Decision 90 — no test file was touched this session), TypeScript **clean**, Copy Quality Guard **PASS — 0 violations across 242 files**, production build **succeeds**.

**Four Mock security layers, assessed:**
- **Layer 1** (`ali_question_bank.mock_eligible` RLS firewall): **PROVEN.** Unchanged since Decision 87; re-confirmed structurally this session by the still-passing migration 069 policy tests.
- **Layer 2** (`ali_mock_form` sealed-manifest firewall): **PROVEN**, per the Founder-supplied catalogue evidence that `ali_mock_form_select_all` no longer exists and only the admin-write policy remains. Evidence supplied as summarized conclusions rather than a literal raw catalogue query/result pair (contrast Decision 87/90's verbatim `pg_policies` rows) — not independently re-queried this session; see evidence-quality note below.
- **Layer 3** (attempt-scoped RPC authorization/ownership): **PROVEN**, per the Founder-supplied `EXECUTE`-grant evidence plus the 12 reported live behavioural checks. Same evidence-form caveat as Layer 2 — not independently re-executed this session.
- **Layer 4** (serialized learner payload field-level redaction): **PROVEN**, on the strongest evidence tier supplied this reconciliation — the literal raw RPC JSON payload, quoted above, plus recursive inspection finding zero protected fields — directly consistent with this session's own re-run of the redaction-projection test.

**Contamination checks reported and structurally consistent:** no Practice eligibility change, no Mock eligibility change, no mastery evidence, no durable educational evidence, no readiness evidence, no parent-report evidence, no Today's Mission contamination. Consistent with migration 071's own scope (grants + one policy only, confirmed this session by the still-passing test *"the migration never changes `ali_question_bank.eligibility_status`"*) and the unchanged production counts above.

**Defects discovered this reconciliation:** none new — Findings A and B were already discovered and closed by migration 071 in Decision 90. **Defects corrected this reconciliation:** none required; no code, test, or migration file was changed this session.

**Evidence-quality observation (not a blocking finding):** Layers 2 and 3's supporting evidence this session was supplied as narrative conclusions rather than the literal raw catalogue query text and result rows Decisions 87 and 90 used verbatim. Layer 4's payload and the production counts were supplied in that stronger, literal form. Recommend future closure reconciliations retain the exact query text and literal row output for Layers 2/3 as well, so the permanent record stays independently auditable rather than resting on a paraphrase — same evidentiary discipline this project already applies elsewhere, not a new standard.

**What this decision does NOT claim:** it does not claim this session independently queried production (no DB credential available in this environment); it does not claim any new code, test, or migration was written or applied this session; it does not claim 008E, real Mock content, Forms A/B/C, or any Mock visual work has begun.

**Verdict:** 008D is genuinely **CLOSED** — on the basis of (a) Founder-supplied Level 1 evidence covering every item Decision 90 left open, internally consistent with no contradictions found against this repository's own migration/test history, and (b) this session's own independent re-execution of the full test suite, TypeScript, Copy Quality Guard, and production build, all clean.

**Date:** 2026-08-18

**Rationale:** The fixture-gated live redaction proof was the single item Decision 90 left open, fully specified in advance (exact insert/cleanup SQL, every object created, post-cleanup zero-row verification) and explicitly gated on migration 071's own application first. The evidence reported this session follows that exact pre-agreed sequence and plan, with no deviation, no scope creep, and no new finding — the closure this decision records is the direct, expected conclusion of a plan this project already committed to in writing before any fixture was created, not a fresh certification invented at closure time.

### Decision 92 — Programme Increment 008V: Mock Visual and Product Experience Standard V1 produced — research, benchmarking, and product architecture only, no implementation; one CRITICAL architectural finding surfaced (not a security defect)

**Decision:** Per Founder authorisation, produced `ANGEL_008V_MOCK_VISUAL_PRODUCT_EXPERIENCE_STANDARD_V1.md` covering: a direct codebase reconstruction of the current Mock experience; refreshed official CSSE exam facts (read directly from csse.org.uk and the full 16-page CSSE Information Guide 2027 Entry PDF); a UK 11+ competitor benchmark; a broader digital-assessment UX benchmark; and 17 further design/architecture sections (Mock journey, workspace standard, English/Maths standards, visual design, device standard, accessibility, timer, Mock-type taxonomy, personalisation boundary, results experience, Exam Intelligence integration, exam-update architecture, anti-memorisation standard, premium differentiation, visual concept, acceptance standard, prioritised gap analysis). No code, migration, or test file was written or changed. No Mock content was created. No question was marked `mock_eligible`. Forms A/B/C were not created. The secure 008D delivery architecture was not modified.

**Codebase reconstruction finding (CRITICAL, carried into the gap analysis, not a security defect):** the live "Start mock" entry point a real user reaches today (`app/learning-intelligence/mock-exam/page.tsx`, linked from `app/mocks/page.tsx`) is architecturally disconnected from the only proven-secure engine (`lib/mockAttempt/client.ts`, the 5 RPCs proven across Decisions 87–91). It calls `fetchMockEligibleQuestionBank()` (`lib/ali/questionBank.ts:112-131`), an unprojected `.select("*")` against `ali_question_bank`, then grades client-side. This is confirmed **not** a live security defect — Decision 87's RLS predicate on `ali_question_bank_select_all` blocks any non-admin `mock_eligible` read regardless of this page's own query, and Mock Eligible remains 0 — so the directive's own "document and STOP" clause for a genuine security defect was not triggered. It is instead a structural product dead-end: once real Mock content exists, this route will silently return zero rows to any real learner. Recorded as the top CRITICAL item for 008E, with an explicit recommendation not to build further on this runner.

**Official CSSE evidence refreshed**, with one load-bearing confirmed change: effective September 2024 (2025 entry onward), the CSSE English paper no longer contains Applied Reasoning questions — confirmed directly from csse.org.uk. Paper structure (two papers, English 60+10 min, Maths 60 min, each standardised and weighted 50%), permitted equipment, results/re-marking policy, and the full 2027-entry cycle date sequence were confirmed directly from the official Information Guide PDF, read in full (16/16 pages) after this session's own tooling (pypdf, since neither this session's Read tool nor the research fork's fetch tooling could parse the PDF directly — poppler-utils is not installed in this environment) succeeded where the research fork's own attempt could not. Two items remain genuinely unconfirmed and are recorded as open, not guessed: the exact timed placement of Continuous Writing within the English paper, and the precise English question-format mix — the official Information Guide does not publish this level of detail, confirmed by reading it in full rather than inferred from a failed fetch.

**Competitor and broader-UX benchmarks** were conducted via three parallel research forks, each returning evidence tagged DIRECT/marketing-copy-level observation vs. INFERENCE, with no proprietary visual design described for copying. One competitor (BOFA) was found to already enforce, in a shipped product, the identical sealed-Mock-vs-adaptive-practice boundary this directive itself specifies — independent external corroboration that the Founder's own boundary is a real, defensible product distinction, not merely an internal caution.

**Process note:** one of the four research forks initially misread its own role — on its first turn it echoed back the coordinating session's own "wait for the other forks" language as if it were its own plan, rather than performing its assigned codebase-reconstruction task. This was caught immediately (the fork's result contained no actual findings) and corrected by resuming it with an explicit statement of its actual identity and task; its second turn produced the full, correct Part 1 findings used in this decision. Recorded here because it is a genuine anomaly in how this session's own tooling behaved, not because it affected any evidence in the final document — no finding in this decision or the standard document rests on the fork's confused first turn.

**Verdict:** 008V research/standard deliverable is complete and returned to the Founder for review. It does **not** constitute 008E approval — the standard's own Part 22 gap analysis makes a scoping recommendation for 008E, but scope commitment remains the Founder's decision.

**Date:** 2026-08-18

**Rationale:** The Founder's own directive drew a hard line between this research/architecture increment and any implementation increment, consistent with this project's established Blueprint → Validation → Implementation discipline. Producing a Founder-facing standard that is itself evidence-tagged throughout (OFFICIAL EVIDENCE / COMPETITOR OBSERVATION / PRODUCT INFERENCE / FOUNDER REQUIREMENT / ANGEL 11+ DESIGN DECISION) extends the same evidence discipline already applied to 008D's own closure to a document that is, by its nature, mostly synthesis and judgement rather than verifiable system state — the tagging exists precisely so the Founder can tell which claims are load-bearing fact and which are this session's own recommendation.

**Implications:** No 008E work begins automatically. The Founder must review `ANGEL_008V_MOCK_VISUAL_PRODUCT_EXPERIENCE_STANDARD_V1.md` and make the scope decision for 008E, informed by but not bound to Part 22's recommendation. Two specific items require Founder or direct-CSSE follow-up before any exam-authentic English Mock workspace is finalised: Continuous Writing's exact timed placement, and the English paper's precise question-format mix.

### Decision 93 — Programme Increment 008E, Secure Mock Experience Integration and Reporting Foundation: the canonical learner-facing Mock Exam route now runs entirely on the proven 008D engine, with a new purely-additive migration (072, not applied) establishing the flag/discovery/reporting architecture; the prior client-graded, unfirewalled-by-architecture path is fully retired from this route

**Decision:** Per Founder authorisation ("008V APPROVED, proceed with 008E"), `app/learning-intelligence/mock-exam/page.tsx` — the actual destination `/mocks`' own "Start mock" button links to — was rewritten to use exclusively `lib/mockAttempt/client.ts`'s SECURITY DEFINER RPC wrappers (migration 070, unmodified) plus three new ones (migration 072, NOT APPLIED, generated for Founder review). It no longer imports `lib/ali/questionBank.ts` at all — the prior `fetchMockEligibleQuestionBank()` → raw `.select("*")` against `ali_question_bank` → client-side grading path (`checkMathsAnswer`/`scoreEnglishAnswer`/writing-LLM-grading) is fully removed from this route, not bypassed or duplicated alongside it.

**Migration 072, purely additive, zero changes to the 5 proven functions.** Adds: `ali_mock_attempt_flag` (learner-owned flag state, same four-guard-check discipline as `mock_submit_answer`); `ali_mock_attempt_report` (the reserved shape for 008E's own required result data contract — `scoring_state`/`analysis_state`/`report_release_state` plus nullable JSON/text columns for overall performance, subject breakdown, question-level outcome, competency evidence, strengths, weaknesses, timing evidence, Practice-vs-Mock comparison, parent explanation — every data column stays null, no score or readiness figure is invented); a trigger (`mock_attempt_report_init_trigger`, fires only on the pre-existing `ali_mock_attempt.status` column's transition into `'submitted'`) that creates a bare report row without ever touching `mock_submit_attempt()` itself — proving structurally that submission and report availability are different events, per the directive's own instruction; `mock_get_active_form()` (redacted — returns only `form_id`/`attempt_type`, never `question_manifest`, closing the real discovery gap migration 071 left: with `ali_mock_form_select_all` dropped, no client-facing way existed to learn which form to request); `mock_get_attempt_manifest()` (returns the caller's own frozen `assigned_question_ids`, IDs only — added as an RPC rather than the direct `.from()` read RLS would already permit, purely to preserve this codebase's own documented "every Mock interaction goes through `supabase.rpc()`" convention); `mock_set_flag()`. All three new functions: `authenticated`-only execute grants, never `anon` — verified structurally.

**Deliberately out of scope, disclosed, not silently dropped:**
- **No scoring/analysis logic was built.** The report row stays `not_started`/`pending` indefinitely until a dedicated future increment builds the real pipeline. This is the directive's own explicit instruction ("submission and full diagnostic feedback are not necessarily the same event"), not an oversight.
- **The old page's deep integration — the adaptive paper builder, the Educational Intelligence evidence pipeline (`processEvidenceForCompetency`), `recordReadinessSnapshot`, `saveMockResult`/`lib/mockProgress.ts`, the legacy `recordSkillResult` bridge, and the Standard/Adaptive mode selector — is not carried into the new page.** All of that machinery existed to turn an immediate client-side grade into evidence; with grading removed from this route (by the directive's own design), none of it has real correctness data to operate on. Carrying it forward as dead/simulated code would have been worse than removing it. **Named consequence:** once real Mock content exists, a completed Full CSSE Mock will not yet update the Learning Report, readiness, or Mock History/best-score display (`/mocks`' own local-storage-backed panel, untouched by this decision) the way the old page did — that capability returns only once a scoring/analysis increment consumes the new report architecture. Today this has zero live effect (Mock Eligible is 0; no learner could complete a real attempt through either the old or new implementation), but it is a real, disclosed gap for the next increment to close, not a silent regression.
- **008V Part 6's real two-paper (English 60+10min reading, then a break, then Mathematics 60min) section-timing model was not built.** `mock_start_attempt()` still provides one server-authoritative timer for the whole attempt; the new page uses it as-is. A section-transition RPC would be needed for the real structure and was not built this increment.
- **008V Part 7's split-screen English passage treatment was not built.** `question` payloads render safely for a plain string or unrecognised rich content only.
- `app/mocks/adaptive/{gl,english,maths,vocabulary}/page.tsx` were left untouched — 008V's own Part 13 taxonomy classifies these as adaptive practice runners, not the formal Mock Exam entry point the directive named, and touching them was judged outside this increment's scope. `tests/lib/ali/mockContentFirewall.test.ts`'s original test 5 (source-check on `fetchMockEligibleQuestionBank` usage) still applies to these four files, unmodified; a new test 5b was added specifically for `mock-exam/page.tsx`, asserting the stronger guarantee (no `lib/ali/questionBank` import at all) that now actually describes it.

**Verification performed this session:**
- Full suite: **592/592 pass** (566 baseline + 24 new migration/workspace tests + 2 net from splitting the firewall test). TypeScript clean. ESLint clean (one real bug caught and fixed: `handleSubmit` was referenced before its `useCallback` declaration inside the timer effect — reordered). Copy Quality Guard PASS after fixing 4 genuine em-dash violations this session's own new learner-facing copy introduced (245 files, 0 violations). Production build succeeds; `/learning-intelligence/mock-exam` still statically generates.
- Safe, read-only live checks via the anon key (no writes, no fixture, nothing to clean up): anon sees 0 `mock_eligible` rows on `ali_question_bank` (consistent with, though not independently re-proving beyond, the Founder-attested true 0 from Decision 91); anon cannot read `ali_mock_form` at all (0 rows, no error — the expected RLS-denial-by-absence behaviour from migration 071); calling `mock_get_active_form` fails with "function not found," confirming migration 072 is genuinely not yet applied, matching its own header.
- Per this project's own established practice (Decisions 86/87/89/90/91), production/security state beyond what the anon key can read requires the Founder's own Level 1 evidence — not available this session, not fabricated here.

**Stop condition assessed:** no genuine security defect was found. The disconnected-old-path finding 008V's own Decision 92 flagged CRITICAL was a product dead-end, not a security defect (RLS already backstopped it) — this decision's own rewrite resolves it by replacement, not by a security fix.

**Live production fixture verification, not performed, deliberately:** proving the full new flow end-to-end (discover → create → start → answer → flag → review → submit → report-row-created) against a real attempt requires an active `ali_mock_form` row, which Content Boundary forbids this session from creating, and requires migration 072 to be applied first, which only the Founder can do. The directive's own STOP condition applies here: this is a genuinely new fixture-and-verification need migration 070's own already-approved protocol (`scripts/verify-mock-attempt-engine.mjs`) does not cover (it never exercises `mock_get_active_form`, `mock_get_attempt_manifest`, `mock_set_flag`, or the report-init trigger). An exact, ready-to-run extension of that same script is offered to the Founder for their own optional live verification after migrations 072 is applied — not executed by this session.

**What this decision does NOT claim:** it does not claim migration 072 has been applied; it does not claim a real Mock attempt has ever been completed through the new page; it does not claim the reporting architecture has been exercised live; it does not claim Mock Eligible is anything other than 0; it does not claim any real Mock content, Form A/B/C, or scoring logic exists; it does not claim `mocks/adaptive/*` were reviewed or changed; it does not claim the two-paper section-timing model or split-screen English treatment (008V Parts 6/7) are built.

**Verdict:** 008E's Mandatory First Priority is resolved for the one named route (`/learning-intelligence/mock-exam`): it no longer depends on `fetchMockEligibleQuestionBank()` → direct `ali_question_bank` retrieval → client-side grading, and cannot become a second, parallel "valid" Mock engine, because the prior implementation was replaced in place, not left running alongside the new one. The lifecycle/reporting architecture required by the directive is established at the schema level, unexercised. 008E's own directive draws a hard line against 008F beginning automatically; this decision does not cross it.

**Date:** 2026-08-18

**Rationale:** The directive named a precise, narrow Mandatory First Priority (retire the insecure path on the real learner-facing route) ahead of the broader reporting-foundation work, and separately warned against turning 008E into a full visual redesign or a second scoring engine. Replacing the route in place, building only the schema/RPC architecture the directive's own Part 5-7 items require (never the scoring computation itself), and disclosing every dropped integration explicitly rather than silently degrading it, follows both constraints at once: the security priority is resolved now, and the harder, riskier work (real grading logic, real section timing, real passage layout) is named as future scope rather than rushed into this increment under time pressure.

**Implications:** No 008F work begins automatically. Before any real Mock content, Form A/B/C, or scoring logic is built, the Founder should decide: (1) whether to apply migration 072 and, optionally, run the offered extended live-verification script; (2) whether the disclosed loss of Learning-Report/readiness/Mock-History integration for CSSE Mock attempts is acceptable until a dedicated scoring/analysis increment restores it, or whether that increment should be prioritised before any real content is created; (3) whether `mocks/adaptive/*`'s continued use of the pre-008E content-retrieval pattern needs its own remediation, given it was named a residual gap here rather than fixed.

### Decision 94 — Programme Increment 008E, Post-Migration Production Verification: migration 072 confirmed installed and behaviourally correct on every dimension except one; a genuine, bounded execute-grant defect discovered on all 3 new functions (anon can invoke them, matching the exact defect class Decision 89 found and 071 fixed) — 008E is NOT genuinely closed; STOPPED per directive, no fix applied

**Decision:** Following the Founder's confirmation that migration 072 was manually applied to production, this session performed bounded, read-only-except-where-explicitly-authorised verification. **A genuine defect was found and this session stopped, reported it, and changed nothing**, per the directive's own explicit instruction ("If a security or architectural defect is discovered, STOP and report it before changing anything").

**The defect:** `mock_get_active_form`, `mock_get_attempt_manifest`, and `mock_set_flag` (all three new functions from migration 072) are callable by a bare, fully unauthenticated `anon`-key client with no Supabase Auth session at all — proven behaviourally, not inferred: a fresh client (anon key, `signInAnonymously()` never called) received a clean response from all three RPCs rather than a permission-denied error. `mock_get_attempt_manifest` and `mock_set_flag` failed safely with "Attempt ... not found for caller" (their own internal `profile_id = v_profile_id` check correctly finds no match when `auth.uid()` is null) — bounded, not exploitable, exactly Decision 89's own "Finding A" reasoning. **`mock_get_active_form` has no identity check at all by design** (it was never meant to need one — a form's own id was reasoned to be non-sealed information) and returned a **clean, non-error response** (`[]`, since no active form currently exists) rather than being denied outright.

**Root cause, matching a known defect class exactly:** migration 072's own grant statements (`revoke all on function ... from public; grant execute on function ... to authenticated;`) reproduce precisely the pattern migration 070 originally used and Decision 89 found insufficient — a Supabase project-level default-privilege grant to `anon` survives a bare `revoke ... from public`, because `REVOKE FROM PUBLIC` only removes the `PUBLIC` pseudo-grant, never an explicit per-role grant made via `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES`. Migration 071 fixed this for the original 5 functions with an explicit `revoke execute on function ... from anon` statement for each. **Migration 072 omitted that same explicit anon-revoke for all 3 of its own new functions** — this session's own defect, introduced when writing 072, not a pre-existing platform issue and not something 008D's own architecture caused.

**Practical impact, precisely bounded:** today, zero. No active Mock form exists (Content Boundary correctly holding — see Mock Eligible confirmation below), so `mock_get_active_form` currently returns an empty result to anyone, authenticated or not. `mock_create_attempt` (unaffected, migration 070's own function, correctly still `authenticated`-only per this session's own direct re-test) remains the actual gate on ever creating a real attempt, so no attempt/answer/flag data is reachable via this gap regardless. **The defect becomes live-impactful the moment a real Mock form is ever activated**: at that point, an anonymous, never-signed-in caller would be able to learn a currently-active form's `form_id` and `attempt_type` without authenticating at all — a real, if narrow, unauthenticated information disclosure (which Mock is currently live and its type), and a violation of this codebase's own stated, consistent design intent ("authenticated only, never anon" — the same rule every other Mock RPC in this codebase, migrations 070/071 both, already correctly enforces).

**Not fixed this session, deliberately, per the directive's own STOP instruction.** The evident fix (three explicit `revoke execute on function public.mock_get_active_form(text)/mock_get_attempt_manifest(uuid)/mock_set_flag(uuid,text,boolean) from anon;` statements, mirroring migration 071's own established remediation exactly) is not applied, drafted as a migration file, or committed in this session — reported for Founder decision first.

**Everything else verified this session, independently, with the evidence tier stated:**
- **Table/trigger existence — behavioural, via anon key (relation-not-found vs. RLS-empty-result are distinguishable via the anon key even without catalogue access):** `ali_mock_attempt_report` and `ali_mock_attempt_flag` both exist (queries succeeded, no "relation does not exist" error), alongside the three pre-existing Mock tables, all unaffected.
- **The 5 proven 008D RPCs — behavioural, re-tested directly against production:** all 5 correctly return "permission denied for function ..." to a bare anon-key caller, unchanged and unaffected by 072, exactly as migration 071 established.
- **Production content counts — independently computed, not merely accepted from the stated baseline, via `count: 'exact'` queries the anon key's own RLS permits for every non-`mock_eligible` status:** TOTAL 312, Practice Eligible 295 (Maths 175, English 120), Provisional 17, `independently_validated` 0 — **an exact match to the established baseline, computed directly by this session, not merely repeated.** Mock Eligible: anon sees 0 (RLS-filtered — this specific figure is not independently provable beyond the anon key's own boundary, same caveat as every prior decision in this chain).
- **Practice regression — behavioural, direct read against production:** a real Maths `practice_eligible` row returns its `answer` field; a real English `practice_eligible` row returns `modelAnswer`/`acceptedAnswers`/passage content; a `practice_eligible` row with `workingSteps` present was found and readable. No regression from migration 072.
- **Formal Mock entry journey — behavioural, via direct RPC calls matching exactly what the page's own code invokes, not a live browser click-through (no confirmed production URL was available to this session, and none was guessed — see this decision's own "Evidence limitations" below):** `mock_get_active_form('full_mock')` returns an empty, error-free result when authenticated (via the extended verification script's own first three steps, which ran successfully up to that exact point) — this is the correct, honest "no mock available" behaviour the new page's own code is built to handle, with no crash and no fallback to the retired path (confirmed by this session's own prior source-level test, `mockContentFirewall.test.ts`'s test 5b, still passing).
- **Extended verification script — run this session per explicit Founder authorisation ("Run: the extended 008E verification script"):** stopped at its own step 3 ("No active form found for attempt_type = 'full_mock'"), exactly as expected since no fixture form was inserted. Its own first two steps did write real, if inert, production rows before stopping: one throwaway `auth.users` identity and one throwaway `profiles` row (name = "008E verification (throwaway)"). **This session could not retrieve their exact IDs after the fact** (a fresh, unauthenticated anon-key query cannot read another identity's own `profiles` row — RLS correctly denies it, the same protection this whole architecture relies on) — the Founder can locate them via the Supabase Dashboard by that exact name string and a `created_at` timestamp matching this session. No attempt/answer/flag/report row was created (the script never reached that step).
- **Automated verification — re-run fresh this session:** 592/592 tests, TypeScript clean, ESLint clean, Copy Quality Guard PASS (0/245 files), production build succeeds. No file was changed this session (`git status` clean throughout) — no new commit.

**What this decision does NOT claim:** it does not claim 008E is closed; it does not claim the execute-grant defect has been fixed; it does not claim the throwaway verification identity has been cleaned up; it does not claim a live browser journey was walked through; it does not claim Mock Eligible's true production value beyond what anon's own RLS-bounded view can show; it does not claim `mocks/adaptive/*` was reviewed (unchanged from Decision 93, still named as residual technical debt, not fixed).

**Verdict:** 008E is **NOT genuinely closed.** Every verification item this session could check independently passed, except one genuine, bounded, currently-zero-impact but real defect in this session's own migration 072, which must be fixed and re-verified before closure.

**Date:** 2026-08-18

**Rationale:** The Founder's own closure rule for this session was explicit: do not declare 008E closed merely because migration 072 ran successfully; closure requires the deployed architecture and security behaviour to actually reconcile. One did not. Reporting this precisely and stopping, rather than quietly patching it inline under the same authorisation that asked for verification, keeps the same discipline this project has applied at every prior closure gate (Decisions 86, 89 both found a genuine finding at a "verification" step and treated it as blocking, not folded silently into the same pass).

**Implications:** No 008F work begins. Before 008E can close, the Founder should: (1) authorise the drafting and application of a small, migration-071-pattern fix (explicit anon-revoke on the 3 new functions); (2) re-run this session's own behavioural anon-access checks (or the consolidated SQL query below) to confirm the fix; (3) clean up the one throwaway `auth.users`/`profiles` pair this session's own explicitly-authorised script run created, via the Supabase Dashboard, by name.

**Consolidated catalogue-evidence SQL query, for the Founder's own Supabase SQL Editor** (per the directive's own instruction to provide exactly one consolidated query rather than several small ones) — confirms or disproves this decision's own behavioural finding at the catalogue level, plus every other Part 1 item this session could not verify without authenticated access:

```sql
select 'A. table/column' as check_group, table_name as k1, column_name as k2, data_type as k3, is_nullable as k4, coalesce(column_default, '') as k5
from information_schema.columns
where table_schema = 'public' and table_name in ('ali_mock_attempt_report', 'ali_mock_attempt_flag')

union all

select 'B. rls_enabled', c.relname, c.relrowsecurity::text, c.relforcerowsecurity::text, '', ''
from pg_class c join pg_namespace n on n.oid = c.relnamespace
where n.nspname = 'public' and c.relname in ('ali_mock_attempt_report', 'ali_mock_attempt_flag')

union all

select 'C. policy', tablename, policyname, roles::text, cmd, coalesce(qual, '')
from pg_policies
where schemaname = 'public' and tablename in ('ali_mock_attempt_report', 'ali_mock_attempt_flag')

union all

select 'D. trigger', trigger_name, event_manipulation, event_object_table, action_timing, ''
from information_schema.triggers
where trigger_schema = 'public' and trigger_name = 'mock_attempt_report_init_trigger'

union all

select 'E. function_grant -- THE CRITICAL CHECK: any row here with grantee = anon on mock_get_active_form/mock_get_attempt_manifest/mock_set_flag confirms this decision''s own behavioural finding', routine_name, grantee, privilege_type, '', ''
from information_schema.routine_privileges
where routine_schema = 'public'
  and routine_name in ('mock_get_active_form', 'mock_get_attempt_manifest', 'mock_set_flag', 'mock_create_attempt', 'mock_start_attempt', 'mock_get_question', 'mock_submit_answer', 'mock_submit_attempt')

order by 1, 2, 3;
```

### Decision 95 — Programme Increment 008E, Security Defect Correction (migration 073, not applied) plus a read-only anonymous-auth-user investigation: root cause proven at catalogue level by Founder-supplied evidence, the proven migration-071 fix pattern applied unchanged, and the ~59 Authentication-panel anonymous users traced to the product's own core learner-identity mechanism, not primarily verification residue

**Decision:** The Founder supplied verbatim `information_schema.routine_privileges` catalogue evidence confirming Decision 94's own behavioural finding exactly: `anon` holds `EXECUTE` on `mock_get_active_form`, `mock_get_attempt_manifest`, and `mock_set_flag`, alongside `authenticated`/`postgres`/`service_role` on all three. Migration 073 (NOT APPLIED, generated for Founder review) applies the identical, already-proven migration-071 remediation — three explicit `revoke execute on function ... from anon` statements, nothing else. No function body is redefined; no RLS policy, table, trigger, or the 5 proven 008D RPCs are touched.

**Regression tests added** (`tests/supabase/mockLifecycleFunctionsRevokeAnon.test.ts`, 9 tests, mirroring `mockAttemptFunctionsRevokeAnon.test.ts`'s own established pattern for migration 071): confirm exactly 3 revoke-from-anon statements with the correct signatures; confirm `authenticated` is never revoked; confirm none of the 5 proven RPCs are mentioned at all; confirm no function body redefinition, no RLS policy touched, no table/content/`eligibility_status` touched, no report-init trigger or new-072-table object touched; confirm the migration's own header quotes the Founder-supplied catalogue evidence verbatim, as the disclosed basis for the correction.

**Regression results:** 601/601 tests pass (592 + 9 new). TypeScript clean. Copy Quality Guard PASS (0/245). Production build succeeds. `npx eslint .` (whole repository, unscoped) reports 62 pre-existing errors and 18 warnings — independently confirmed, via `git status`/`git diff`, to be entirely in files this session did not touch (e.g. `components/providers/AuthProvider.tsx`, `scripts/generate-icons.js`, `tests/content/007xBatch.test.ts`) and unrelated to 008E; the two files this session actually added (`supabase/migrations/073_...sql`, `tests/supabase/mockLifecycleFunctionsRevokeAnon.test.ts`) lint clean when scoped directly. Not fixed here — out of this correction's own tightly bounded scope ("no unrelated refactoring").

**Production-content impact:** none. Migration 073 touches no table, no row, no `eligibility_status`. Mock Eligible remains 0 (unchanged from every prior decision in this chain). No Form A/B/C, no scoring logic, no visual-standard work.

**Second finding — anonymous-auth-user investigation, read-only, no deletion, no modification performed:**

Traced every use of `signInAnonymously()` in the repository. **Primary, dominant finding: this is not chiefly verification/test residue — it is the product's own core, load-bearing learner-identity mechanism**, by design, since the ED-001 permanent correction (2026-07-23, `ARCH-001_ED-001_IDENTITY_CORRECTION_IMPLEMENTATION.md`):

- `lib/learnerIdentity.ts`'s `ensureLearnerSession()` is, by its own header comment, "the single reusable entry point for who is the current learner" — it calls Supabase's real `signInAnonymously()`, creating a genuine `auth.users` row with `role: authenticated`, `is_anonymous: true`, whenever no session exists.
- `components/providers/AuthProvider.tsx` — mounted at the application root (`app/layout.tsx:75`, wrapping every single page) — calls `ensureLearnerSession()` automatically on mount for any visitor with no existing session. This means **every browser/device that ever loads any page of the live application, without ever completing a magic-link login, receives its own real, permanent `auth.users` row**, by design — not a leak, not an accident.
- `lib/supabaseProgress.ts`'s `ensureProfile()` — called throughout the app (every Practice page, the new Mock Exam page, etc.) — also calls `ensureLearnerSession()` and then creates a matching `profiles` row on first use.

**Classification:** predominantly **C (application-generated anonymous identities)**, overlapping with **A (expected legitimate users)** — a real prospective family/child trying the app without yet logging in is, by design, exactly this kind of user, and this is the intended, correct low-friction first-use behaviour, not a defect. A real, smaller, separately-identifiable contribution is **B (verification/test residue)**: `scripts/verify-mock-attempt-engine.mjs` (008D) and `scripts/verify-mock-lifecycle-and-reporting-foundation.mjs` (008E, run this session per explicit Founder authorisation in the prior turn) each call `signInAnonymously()` plus a `profiles` insert, with no automated cleanup step in either script — by the same established pattern Decision 91's own diagnostic-identity cleanup addressed for a single 008D-era row. `scripts/test-learner-identity.ts` was checked and confirmed to use a fake in-memory client only — it creates no real production identity. No category **D** finding was needed; the evidence was decisive enough to classify without inferring individual account ownership.

**Whether anonymous auth is still required:** yes, unconditionally — it is the sole mechanism by which an unauthenticated learner's Practice/Mock activity can be tied to a real, RLS-verifiable `auth.uid()` at all (replacing the pre-ED-001 client-supplied `device_id`, which RLS could never verify). Disabling it would break the app for every learner who has not completed a magic-link login.

**Whether any cleanup/consolidation mechanism exists:** no. `claim_legacy_profile()` (migration 019) only reassigns a `profiles` row whose `auth_user_id IS NULL` — a pre-ED-001 legacy state. Since ED-001, every new profile is created with a real, non-null `auth_user_id` from the moment of its first `ensureProfile()` call, so `claim_legacy_profile()` structurally cannot apply to any post-ED-001 anonymous-then-later-login case. **A genuine, separate product gap follows from this** (named here, not fixed — out of this correction's scope): if a learner uses the app anonymously first and later logs in via magic link, `ensureProfile()` finds no profile for the new real `auth_user_id`, `claim_legacy_profile()` finds nothing to claim (the old profile is already non-null-owned, by the anonymous identity), and a **second, separate profile is created** — the anonymous identity's own progress/history is silently orphaned, not merged. This is a real data-continuity gap worth the Founder's own attention in a future increment, independent of the auth-user-count question this investigation was asked to answer.

**Whether these accounts can have linked profiles, learning history, attempts, mastery, audit evidence, or other durable records:** yes, unambiguously — this is the exact mechanism every real learner's real progress is stored under. Per the finding immediately above, an anonymous identity can be the **sole, permanent home** of a real learner's entire history if they never log in, or of their pre-login history if they later do. This is precisely why the Founder's own instruction ("do not infer ownership from creation dates alone," "any deletion must require evidence-backed Founder authorisation") is the correct standard, not excess caution — Decision 91's own 14-table check, applied to exactly one identity that session, would need to be applied individually to any candidate row here before deletion.

**Safe reconciliation/cleanup proposal — proposed only, not executed:**
1. **Do not bulk-delete by age, count, or the "Anonymous" provider label alone** — any of these rows may hold real, durable learner data.
2. **The narrowly-identifiable verification-residue rows are safe to target specifically**, by the exact same evidence-first protocol Decision 91 already established: query `profiles` for `name = '008D verification (throwaway)'` and `name = '008E verification (throwaway)'` (the two exact strings these two scripts use), independently verify all dependent tables are 0 for each matched `profile_id` (the same 14-table list Decision 91 checked), and only then delete via Supabase Dashboard → Authentication → Users (never raw SQL), exactly as Decision 91 did.
3. **For the broader pool, recommend the Founder check Supabase's own project-level anonymous-user settings** (some plans offer automatic anonymous-session expiry/cleanup) before any custom mechanism is built — this may already be a solved problem at the platform level, not something this codebase needs to build.
4. **Recommend a future, separately-scoped increment** to build a real anonymous-to-authenticated identity/profile merge (extending ED-001), closing the silent-orphaning gap named above — a product-integrity improvement, not a cleanup task.
5. Any actual deletion, of any row, requires explicit Founder authorisation per-row or per-precisely-defined-criterion — none is authorised by this decision.

**What this decision does NOT claim:** it does not claim migration 073 has been applied; it does not claim 008E is closed; it does not claim any anonymous user has been deleted, modified, or disabled; it does not claim the true total count or full breakdown of the ~59 accounts (only the Founder's own Dashboard can enumerate them); it does not claim the anonymous-to-authenticated merge gap has been fixed.

**Verdict:** The migration-072 execute-grant defect is corrected in migration 073, pending Founder application. The anonymous-auth-user investigation found the accumulation to be substantially expected, given the product's own real architecture — not primarily a leak — with one small, precisely-identifiable, already-proposed-not-executed cleanup opportunity, and one genuine, separate product gap (silent progress-orphaning on login) named for future attention.

**Date:** 2026-08-18

**Rationale:** The Founder's own directive drew the same hard line here it has drawn throughout this program: fix exactly the proven defect, using the exact already-proven pattern, and treat the second finding (anonymous users) as investigation-only until evidence, not inference, supports any deletion. Tracing `signInAnonymously()` to its real, single root cause in `lib/learnerIdentity.ts` and `AuthProvider.tsx` — rather than assuming the Founder's own worry (that verification scripts caused this) was correct — is the more useful, more honest answer even though it's not the simpler one; a wrong root-cause guess here could have led toward disabling or "cleaning up" something structurally load-bearing to how every real learner is identified.

**Implications:** No 008F work begins. Before 008E can close, the Founder must: (1) apply migration 073 via Supabase Dashboard SQL Editor; (2) re-run the same catalogue query from Decision 94 (or an equivalent) to confirm `anon` now holds zero `EXECUTE` grants on the 3 functions while `authenticated`/`postgres`/`service_role` are unchanged, and that the 5 proven 008D RPCs remain correct; (3) request the same behavioural anonymous-call re-test this session already knows how to run. Separately, and not blocking 008E's own closure: the Founder may authorise the narrowly-scoped verification-residue cleanup proposed above, and should decide whether to prioritise the anonymous-to-authenticated merge gap as a future increment.

### Decision 96 — Programme Increment 008E, Post-073 Security Verification: Founder-supplied catalogue evidence and this session's own independent behavioural re-test agree exactly — zero anon EXECUTE across all 8 Mock RPCs, authenticated access preserved, no regression found; 008E genuinely CLOSED

**Decision:** Following the Founder's confirmation that migration 073 was applied ("Success. No rows returned.") and the Founder's own verbatim `information_schema.routine_privileges` catalogue query across all 8 Mock RPCs (showing `authenticated`/`postgres`/`service_role` on every function and `anon` on none), this session independently re-ran the exact behavioural attack Decision 94 originally used to discover the defect, against the same production project, via the same anon key, with no session.

**Result: identical outcome, both functions failed at the Postgres permission layer, not application logic.** All three previously-vulnerable functions now return `code=42501, "permission denied for function ..."` — the standard PostgreSQL insufficient-privilege SQLSTATE, the same exact error class and message format the 5 proven 008D RPCs have always returned to a bare anon caller. This is decisive: before migration 073, `mock_get_active_form` returned a clean, non-error, empty result to anon (the function body executed); `mock_get_attempt_manifest`/`mock_set_flag` returned an application-level "not found" (the function body executed, then failed its own internal check). After 073, all three are rejected before their bodies ever run — the fix moved the enforcement point from "the function's own logic happens to be safe" to "the database refuses the call outright," exactly matching what migration 071 already established for the original 5 functions, and exactly what this closure's own required verification demanded (not merely an application-level error).

**All 8 Mock RPCs, reconciled to one consistent model:** every function — the 5 from migration 070, the 3 from migration 072 — now grants execute to `authenticated`/`postgres`/`service_role` only, never `anon`. The permission architecture is consistent across the entire Mock RPC surface for the first time since migration 072 was written; no exception, no special case remains.

**Authenticated access:** confirmed by the Founder's own catalogue evidence (`authenticated | EXECUTE` present for all 3 corrected functions) — not re-proven with a new throwaway identity this session, per the directive's own instruction not to create one merely to reprove an already-established fact. The catalogue grant is the direct, sufficient proof: a grant that permits the call is not something a behavioural test can meaningfully add evidence against.

**Formal Mock route:** re-confirmed via the existing structural test (`mockContentFirewall.test.ts`'s own test 5b, still passing) and a direct source grep — `app/learning-intelligence/mock-exam/page.tsx` contains no import of `lib/ali/questionBank`; its only mention of `fetchMockEligibleQuestionBank` is inside a comment describing what was removed. No regression to the retired path.

**Production counts, independently re-computed this session via the anon key's own RLS-permitted reads (not merely repeated from a prior session):** TOTAL 312, Practice Eligible 295 (Maths 175, English 120), Provisional 17 — exact match, unchanged. Mock Eligible: anon sees 0 (RLS-bounded, same standing caveat as every prior decision). `ali_mock_form`: 0 rows visible to anon (unchanged — no real form exists).

**No production data mutation caused by this verification session:** every check this session performed was either a pure read (`.select()`, `count: 'exact'`) or an RPC call that was rejected at the permission layer before any function body executed — no `signInAnonymously()` call, no `profiles` insert, no attempt/answer/flag/report row created. `git status` was clean throughout — no code file was changed this session.

**Migration 073, re-confirmed to contain exactly its intended scope:** the file's own executable SQL (comments stripped) is precisely `begin; revoke execute ... from anon;` ×3; `commit;` — nothing else. No function redefinition, no table, no RLS, no content.

**Automated gates, re-run fresh this session:** 601/601 tests pass. TypeScript clean. Copy Quality Guard PASS (0/245 files). Production build succeeds. ESLint scoped to every file 008E/migration 073 touched: 0 errors, 2 warnings — one is ESLint's own "no config for `.sql` files" notice (meaningless), the other (`_table` unused param in `mockContentFirewall.test.ts`) is a pre-existing warning on a line this session never edited (confirmed already in Decision 93). The repository-wide, unscoped `eslint .` run's 62 pre-existing errors (Decision 95) are not re-litigated here and are not an 008E regression.

**Defects discovered this session:** none new. **Defects corrected this session:** none (073 was already prepared and reported in Decision 95; this session only verified its effect).

**Remaining technical debt, unchanged and explicitly not expanded into this closure:** `mocks/adaptive/*`'s continued use of the pre-008E content-retrieval pattern (Decision 93); no scoring/analysis pipeline exists yet (Decisions 93/94); no two-paper section timing or split-screen English (Decision 93); the anonymous-to-authenticated progress-merge gap (Decision 95) — named again here for continuity, not investigated further, per the Founder's own explicit instruction not to expand this closure into anonymous-user remediation. The two named verification-residue profiles remain cleanup candidates only, not deleted.

**Anonymous-identity finding, disposition:** preserved exactly as Decision 95 recorded it — Angel's learner-identity architecture deliberately creates anonymous Supabase identities as its core mechanism, and currently lacks an anonymous-to-authenticated merge. No deletion, no modification to `AuthProvider`/`learnerIdentity`, no merge logic, no profile-creation change was made or proposed for execution this session.

**All 7 closure-gate conditions assessed:**
- **A** (anon behavioural rejection at the permission layer, all 3 RPCs): **MET** — `code=42501` on all three, independently re-tested this session.
- **B** (Founder catalogue evidence, zero anon EXECUTE across all 8): **MET** — Founder-supplied, verbatim, quoted above.
- **C** (authenticated access preserved): **MET** — catalogue-confirmed.
- **D** (5 proven 008D RPCs unaffected): **MET** — re-tested behaviourally this session, identical `42501` rejection for anon, unchanged from every prior decision.
- **E** (secure formal Mock route intact): **MET** — structural test + direct source check, both re-run this session.
- **F** (all automated gates pass): **MET** — 601/601 tests, TypeScript, Copy Quality Guard, build, and scoped ESLint all clean.
- **G** (no new security or production defect discovered): **MET** — none found this session.

**Verdict:** All 7 conditions are independently satisfied, by a combination of Founder-supplied Level 1 catalogue evidence and this session's own re-executed behavioural and automated verification. **008E is genuinely CLOSED.**

**Date:** 2026-08-18

**Rationale:** This closure follows the same two-leg standard every genuine closure in this programme has required (Decisions 87, 91): Founder-supplied catalogue evidence for what only authenticated Dashboard access can prove, combined with this session's own independent re-execution of everything within its own reach (the exact behavioural attack that found the original defect, re-run against the same production project; every automated gate re-run fresh, not merely cited from a prior pass). Both legs agree, with no contradiction and no new finding.

**Implications:** 008E is closed. Per the Founder's own explicit boundary, no 008F work begins automatically. The recommended next programme decision is the Founder's own to make, informed by the technical debt named above (no scoring/analysis pipeline, no real two-paper timing, `mocks/adaptive/*`'s residual pattern, the anonymous-merge gap) and by 008V's own prior gap analysis (Decision 92) — none of which is committed to or begun here.

### Decision 97 — Programme Increment 008F, Mock Scoring, Analysis and Educational Intelligence Integration: secure server-side marking + admin-gated report release built (migration 074, not applied); a real, load-bearing architectural finding — `ali_student_question_history` has no evidence-provenance dimension — means Mock evidence stays inside its own report row in this increment, never fed into mastery/readiness, per the directive's own named fallback

**Decision:** Built the scoring, reporting, and Mock-evidence-boundary architecture the 008F directive requires, entirely ahead of any real Mock content (Mock Eligible remains 0 throughout). Two parallel reconnaissance passes (Part 1) preceded any implementation: one tracing the existing correctness/marks/scoring code across the repository, one tracing the mastery/readiness/evidence pipeline and locating the three frozen governance documents (`docs/intelligence/ASSESSMENT_BRAIN_V1.md`, `LEARNING_ENGINE_V1.md`, `EDUCATIONAL_INTELLIGENCE_ENGINE_V1.md`, all found exactly as named).

**The decisive finding, load-bearing for this entire increment's design:** `ali_student_question_history` — the table `recordOutcome()`/`processEvidenceForCompetency()` (Practice's own real evidence pipeline) read and write — has **no evidence-provenance column at all**. If a Mock attempt's outcomes were written into that same table through the same path, Mock evidence would become **silently indistinguishable from Practice evidence** — exactly the contamination 008F's own Core Principle forbids ("a high Mock score must not automatically make a competency mastered"). This is not a hypothetical risk named for caution; it is the system's real, current, verified shape. Confirmed directly by reading `recordOutcome()`'s own signature (`lib/ali/history.ts:221-229`) and `processEvidenceForCompetency()`'s own signature (`lib/learningEngine/educationalIntelligenceService.ts:180-187`) — neither carries any source/provenance parameter.

**Design response, following the directive's own explicit fallback instruction** ("If current architecture cannot safely consume Mock evidence yet, build the correct boundary/adapter rather than bypassing it"): this increment does **not** call `recordOutcome`, `processEvidenceForCompetency`, or touch `ali_student_question_history`/`ali_durable_mastery`/`ali_educational_audit` anywhere. Mock evidence is computed and stored **only** inside `ali_mock_attempt_report`, explicitly tagged `source: "mock"`, available for Mock-specific reporting, and not consumed by mastery or readiness until a future, separately-scoped increment gives the shared pipeline a real provenance dimension and can safely merge it. A second, real, previously-written-but-never-implemented governance document was found during reconnaissance — `docs/intelligence/MOCK_ATTEMPT_LEDGER_SPECIFICATION_V1.md` — whose own "Architecture Rules" ("orchestration only," "no duplicate mastery calculations," "no duplicate readiness calculations," "every insight references the existing Educational Intelligence Foundation") independently corroborate this exact boundary; the Ledger itself was never built (no such table exists in any migration), but its own stated rules are honoured here in spirit.

**Official CSSE scoring evidence, carried forward from 008V's own direct verification** (Decision 92, csse.org.uk and the official Information Guide, read in full): the CSSE standardisation formula is **cohort-relative and published only after results are sent each year** ("The formula for calculating the standardised score will be published on the CSSE website shortly after the results are despatched" — the Guide's own words), not a fixed, pre-knowable algorithm. This directly and decisively answers 008F's own Part 2 question — **no legitimate conversion from a raw Mock score to an official CSSE standardised score exists, and none can be constructed** — so every report surface built in this increment states only raw marks/percentage, labelled explicitly as Angel's own preparation intelligence (`OFFICIAL_SCORE_DISCLAIMER`, `lib/mockAttempt/reportCopy.ts`), never an official-score claim.

**Migration 074 (NOT APPLIED), purely additive:** extends `ali_mock_attempt_report` (072) with `marking_version`/`released_at` columns; adds `mock_score_attempt(p_attempt_id uuid)` (SECURITY DEFINER, scores a `submitted`-only, locked attempt, deterministic and idempotent per marking version, reads the real stored answer/marks server-side, never trusts a client-supplied correctness value — its only argument is the attempt id) and `mock_release_report(p_attempt_id uuid)` (SECURITY DEFINER, gated by `is_current_user_admin()` inside the function body — report release cannot be self-authorised by the learner). **Both new functions' `anon` EXECUTE is explicitly revoked in this same migration** — applying, correctly and from the start, the exact lesson migration 072's own omission cost two full closure cycles to fix (Decisions 94-96). Zero changes to any of the 8 proven Mock RPCs, their grants, or any existing RLS policy.

**Marking scope, deliberately conservative and disclosed, not silently narrowed:** auto-marking covers only a single scalar answer compared by numeric equality (the same 0.0001 tolerance `checkMathsAnswer()` already uses, kept in sync by convention) or exact case/whitespace-insensitive string match. Writing/essay content, multi-form (semicolon-delimited alternate) answers, and any answer that doesn't resolve to a clean scalar all route to `requires_manual_marking` — never silently guessed. This is narrower than `checkMathsAnswer()`'s own fuller logic (unit parsing, currency stripping, alternate-answer handling) — a deliberate choice, not an oversight: replicating that fuller logic inside untested PL/pgSQL, with no real Mock content yet to validate against, was judged a real correctness risk not worth taking this increment. A percentage is never computed while any question still awaits manual marking.

**A second real architectural constraint, discovered and disclosed:** `ali_mock_attempt_report`'s own RLS (migration 072) is sealed until `report_release_state = 'released'`, with **no admin bypass clause** — and this deployment has no `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (confirmed, migration 070's own comment). This means nothing outside a SECURITY DEFINER SQL function can read a scored-but-unreleased report to run further TypeScript-side processing. Computing competency-evidence classification (which needs `assessmentBrainMap.ts`'s own `QUESTION_TYPE_PRIMARY_COMPETENCY` mapping, a TypeScript-only artefact) therefore cannot happen automatically in this increment without either provisioning a service-role credential or duplicating that mapping into SQL — a deliberate architecture decision left for a dedicated future increment, not made silently here. `mock_score_attempt()` does include each question's own `questionTypeId` (already client-visible via `mock_get_question()`, not a protected field) in its outcome record specifically so a future increment's classification step can reuse the real mapping rather than a second, duplicated one.

**`lib/mockAttempt/evidenceAdapter.ts`, built and tested regardless:** a real, pure, 9-test-covered TypeScript function (`classifyMockEvidence`, `summariseStrengthsAndPriorities`) proving the classification logic is correct — reuses the single real `QUESTION_TYPE_PRIMARY_COMPETENCY` mapping, tags every entry `source: "mock"`, requires at least 2 questions about the same competency within one attempt before calling it a strength or a priority (a report-layer-only signal, explicitly distinct from and not a substitute for the Educational Intelligence Engine's own Evidence Tier system), and carries no answer/response text. Not wired into an automatic pipeline this increment, per the constraint above — proven correct and ready, not yet safely triggerable end-to-end.

**Reporting surfaces, minimal, real, and tested for language discipline (Part 7/8/9):** `app/learning-intelligence/mock-report/[attemptId]/page.tsx` (child) and `app/learning-intelligence/parent/mock-report/[attemptId]/page.tsx` (parent, six clearly separated sections — Trend deliberately omitted, since it needs comparable evidence across multiple attempts this increment cannot yet produce). Both gate entirely on `report_release_state`, both indistinguishable between "not released yet" and "doesn't exist" by design (`getMockAttemptReport()`'s own doc comment explains why — a caller must never infer sealed-state information from an error shape). `lib/mockAttempt/reportCopy.ts`'s language is directly tested (10 tests) against Part 7's own forbidden phrases ("ready," "guarantee," "will score," "pass," "admission," "fail," "weak," "bad," "poor," "behind") and always includes the official-score disclaimer. Explicitly not the full 008V visual redesign, per the directive's own instruction.

**`app/learning-intelligence/mock-exam/page.tsx` wiring:** `scoreMockAttempt()` is called immediately after a successful `submitMockAttempt()`, fire-and-forget, void return — a background step distinct from submission itself, matching the delayed-report principle already established in 008D/008E.

**Anti-memorisation (Part 12):** not implemented, correctly — reconnaissance confirmed no `family_id`/sibling-question-family concept exists anywhere in the schema (`ali_question_bank` has no such column, confirmed by direct migration inspection). Named as a real, unimplemented, future schema gap, not assumed or worked around.

**Verification performed this session:** 639/639 tests pass (601 baseline + 19 migration-074 structural + 9 evidence-adapter + 10 report-copy). TypeScript clean. ESLint scoped to every 008F-touched file: 0 errors (one pre-existing, unrelated warning in a file this session did not edit). Copy Quality Guard PASS after fixing one real em-dash violation in this session's own new parent-report copy (0/249 files). Production build succeeds; both new dynamic routes (`/learning-intelligence/mock-report/[attemptId]`, `/learning-intelligence/parent/mock-report/[attemptId]`) confirmed present and correctly server-rendered on demand. Safe, read-only anon-key checks: production counts unchanged (TOTAL 312, Mock Eligible 0, anon-visible), and both new RPCs confirmed genuinely not yet deployed ("function not found"), consistent with the migration's own NOT APPLIED status. No production mutation caused by this session.

**Security (Part 14):** structural/code-level verification only in this session — live behavioural proof of `mock_score_attempt`/`mock_release_report`'s own access control (matching the exact anon-behavioural-attack pattern that found the migration-072 defect) requires the Founder to apply migration 074 first, per this project's own established two-phase verification practice (Decisions 94-96). No genuine security defect was found in this session's own review; the anon-execute-revoke pattern was applied correctly and completely, unlike 072's own first pass.

**What this decision does NOT claim:** it does not claim migration 074 has been applied; it does not claim any Mock attempt has ever been scored or released in production; it does not claim Mock evidence has ever reached mastery or readiness (it deliberately does not, and cannot safely yet); it does not claim `analysis_state` ever leaves `not_started` in this increment; it does not claim the competency-evidence adapter is wired into any live pipeline; it does not claim anti-memorisation is implemented; it does not claim `mocks/adaptive/*`, the anonymous-identity gap, or automated report release were addressed (all remain named technical debt, unchanged).

**Verdict:** 008F's architecture is built, tested, and internally coherent with the frozen Educational Intelligence documents and this codebase's own established security conventions. It is genuinely bounded to what the directive itself asked for ahead of real content: raw marking, governed release, and a proven-but-not-yet-wired evidence boundary — not a completed end-to-end mastery-integration pipeline, which two real, disclosed architectural constraints (the provenance gap; the sealed-report-with-no-service-role gap) make unsafe to complete blindly in this same increment.

**Date:** 2026-08-18

**Rationale:** Two genuine, previously-undocumented architectural constraints surfaced during reconnaissance that materially changed this increment's own scope from what a literal reading of the directive's full target lifecycle might suggest. Rather than either bypassing them (writing Mock evidence through the contaminated path, or duplicating the competency map into SQL under time pressure) or silently under-delivering without explanation, both are named precisely, with the exact mechanism, the exact file/line evidence, and the exact future decision each one requires — consistent with this programme's own standing discipline of disclosing scope boundaries rather than quietly narrowing them.

**Implications:** No 008G work begins automatically. Before further Mock-evidence work, the Founder should decide: (1) whether to apply migration 074 and request the post-migration verification cycle this project's own established practice requires (matching 008E's own two-phase pattern); (2) whether to prioritise closing the `ali_student_question_history` provenance gap (a real, disclosed prerequisite before Mock evidence can safely reach mastery/readiness at all) as its own dedicated, carefully-tested increment; (3) whether to provision a service-role credential (enabling safer server-side cross-boundary processing generally, not just for this pipeline) or accept the SQL-duplication tradeoff for competency classification specifically. `mocks/adaptive/*`'s residual pattern and the anonymous-identity findings remain exactly as previously recorded (Decisions 93/95), not revisited here.
